L280: Mass Balance and Metabolism of [14C]X581238 in Male Sprague-Dawley Rats / BLU-R5482AM1	Following single oral or IV administration of 30 mg/kg [14C]pralsetinib, pralsetinib primarily underwent oxidation, N-glucuronidation, and GSH conjugation in male rats. Following oral administration, fecal and urinary excretion accounted for 91.75% and 1.55% of the dose, respectively. No metabolites were detected in plasma. M549a/M549b (45% of urine radioactivity; 0.7% of dose) and M709 (30% of urine radioactivity; 0.5% of dose) were detected in urine. M549b (15% of feces radioactivity; 14% of dose), M549a (6% of feces radioactivity and dose), and M563b/M709 (23% of feces radioactivity; 21% of dose) were detected in feces. Following single IV administration of [14C]pralsetinib, biliary, fecal, and urinary excretion accounted for 43.96%, 37.62%, and 5.44% of the dose in bile duct-cannulated rats, respectively.
L281: Metabolite Profiles of [^{14}\text{C}]BLU-667 in Plasma, Urine, and Feces of Monkeys Following Single Oral Dose of [^{14}\text{C}]BLU-667 / BLU-R9705	
Following single oral administration of 10 mg/kg [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib, the drug primarily underwent oxidative metabolism, glucuronidation, and cysteine conjugation in male monkeys. The majority of radioactivity was excreted in the feces (42-89%); urine accounted for \le 5\% of the dose. M549b (1%) and M549g/M453/M709 (co-eluted at 1.4-9.1%) were detected in plasma at <10\% of plasma radioactivity. M549b (7-29% of urine radioactivity) and M549g/M453/M709 (45-65% of urine radioactivity) were detected in urine at \le 2\% of the dose. M549b was the major metabolite detected in feces (11-21% of feces radioactivity; 6-16% of dose), and M652, M531, M549d, M549e, M549h, and M563b were detected in feces at \le 6\% of the dose (2-9% of feces radioactivity).
M531, M453, M549b, M563b, and M709 were detected in human plasma, urine, and/or feces at levels \le 5\%. There were no major metabolites detected in human plasma, urine, or feces at levels \ge 10\% of radioactivity. Overall, considering all the species tested, there were no human-specific metabolites or metabolites present at significantly higher levels in humans than in animals used for toxicological assessment. In addition, there are no major human metabolites identified. No additional toxicological assessment of metabolites is warranted.

Excretion	
A Pharmacokinetic and Excretion Mass Balance Study of [^{14}\text{C}]BLU-667 in Cynomolgus Monkeys Following a Single Oral (Gavage) Dose / 00124835	
Following single oral administration of 10 mg/kg (100 \mu\text{Ci}/\text{kg}) [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib to male cynomolgus monkeys, [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib equivalents were quantifiable in plasma from 0.25-168 hours post-dose, and the whole blood-to-plasma \text{AUC}_{\text{last}} ratio was 0.825. Pralsetinib plasma exposure (\text{AUC}_{\text{last}} = 29400 \text{ ng}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL}) was generally lower than that of [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib equivalents (\text{AUC}_{\text{last}} = 53200 \text{ ng}\cdot\text{hr}/\text{mL}), suggesting the presence of [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib-derived metabolites. The majority of [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib-derived radioactivity was excreted in the feces, with 3.77% and 71.4% of the total administered [^{14}\text{C}]pralsetinib-derived radioactivity being recovered in urine and feces, respectively, over 168 hours post-dose.
L282: SD = Sprague-Dawley; Hrs = Hours
L283: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L284: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L285: ## The Applicant's Position:
L286: All pivotal toxicology studies were conducted in accordance with GLP and ICH S9 guidance using the proposed clinical regimen of daily oral dosing. Repeated-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys of 28 days and 13 weeks duration to support registration of pralsetinib are summarized below.
L287: ## **A GLP-compliant 28-Day Once Daily Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Pralsetinib in Sprague-Dawley Rats with a 14-Day Recovery Period (Report (b) (4) -124570)**
L288: In the GLP-compliant 28-day repeated-dose toxicology study, pralsetinib was administered at oral doses of 10, 20, 30, and 75 mg/kg/day to Sprague Dawley rats (15 animals/sex/group). Dose-limiting toxicity and severely toxic dose effects in rats at 75 mg/kg/day (450 mg/m2/day) were attributed to inanition, metabolic perturbations, and contributions by hematologic and tissue effects. The severely toxic dose eliciting \ge 10\% lethality in the rat was 30 mg/kg/day (180 mg/m2/day). Off target, nonseverely toxic pharmacologic effects included thickened physeal cartilage (femur), hyperphosphatemia with corresponding mineralization in the glandular stomach, incisor tooth degeneration, and decreased bone marrow cellularity with corresponding lower erythron parameters. These effects were either resolving or resolved after the 14-day recovery period with the exception of incisor tooth degeneration.
L289: ## **A GLP-compliant 13-Week Once Daily Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Pralsetinib in Sprague-Dawley Rats (Report 00124770)**
L290: In the GLP-compliant 13-week repeated-dose toxicology study in rats (10 animals/sex/group), pralsetinib was administered at oral doses of 5, 10, and 20 mg/kg/day. There were no new severely toxic findings, nor was there an increase in the severity of known adverse findings. At the highest dosage administered (20 mg/kg/day), the following new adverse findings were observed: testicular degeneration in males, decreased lymphoid cellularity in the thymus in males and females, and degeneration of the corpus luteum in females. Because of these findings, the NOAEL was 10 mg/kg/day (60 mg/m2/day).
L291: ## *FDA Additional Comment on Rat Toxicology Studies:*
L292: Decreased lymphoid cellularity in the thymus was also seen in the 28-day rat toxicology study. In addition to the new adverse findings identified in the 13-week study highlighted by the Applicant, broken teeth and incisor fractures were present at the 20 mg/kg dose level. There was also an increase in the severity of incisor degeneration in the 13-week study compared to the 28-day study. FDA does not insist on identification of a NOAEL in toxicology studies for oncology drugs but based on decreased weight and testicular findings at doses \ge 10 mg/kg the pralsetinib NOAEL appears to be 5 mg/kg/day (30 mg/m2/day) in the 13-week rat toxicology
L293: study.
L294: ## **A GLP-compliant 28-Day Once Daily Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Pralsetinib in Cynomolgus Monkeys with a 14-Day Recovery Period (Report [b] (4) -124571)**
L295: In the GLP-compliant 28-day repeated-dose toxicology study, pralsetinib was administered at oral doses of 5, 15, and 40 mg/kg/day to Cynomolgus monkeys (5 animals/sex/group). Dosage level of 40 mg/kg/day was lethal. Dosage levels of 5 and 15 mg/kg/day were reduced to 2.5 and 7.5 mg/kg/day due to morbidity/mortality. The lethal dose effects of pralsetinib-related mortality in monkeys at 40 mg/kg/day (480 mg/m2/day) were attributed to bacterial sepsis secondary to gastrointestinal inflammation and/or ulceration. Dose-limiting toxicity in the monkey was attributed to inanition, changes in food consumption, decreased body weights, serum chemistry, hematologic and tissue effects. No functional effects on the cardiovascular system were noted based on ECG analyses and in life examinations. The HNSTD in the monkey was 7.5 mg/kg/day (90 mg/m2/day). Off-target nonseverely toxic pharmacologic effects included decreased bone marrow cellularity, phyeal dysplasia, and decreased cellularity of lymphoid organs which corresponded with changes in the hemogram. All effects in the HNSTD group were resolving or resolved after the 14-day recovery period.
L296: ## **A GLP-compliant 13-Week Once Daily Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of Pralsetinib in Cynomolgus Monkeys (Report 00124768)**
L297: In the GLP-compliant 13-week repeated-dose toxicology study in monkeys (4 animals/sex/group), pralsetinib was administered at oral doses of 2, 5, and 10 mg/kg/day. All findings observed in this study were considered to be nonadverse. The NOAEL was 10 mg/kg/day (120 mg/m2/day).
L298: ## **Additional studies**
L299: Toxicity and toxicokinetic of pralsetinib was studied in non-GLP-compliant 7-day oral gavage studies, which were conducted preparatory to the GLP-compliant 28-day oral gavage studies, in male Sprague Dawley rats and Cynomolgus monkeys. The NOAEL in rats was 15 mg/kg/day (90 mg/m2/day) and in monkeys 10 mg/kg/day (120 mg/m2/day).
L300: ## **FDA Additional Comment on Additional Studies (7-day Toxicity Studies):**
L301: FDA did not review the 7-day toxicology studies in rats, dogs, or monkeys because they are not relevant for this stage of development. Thus, the Applicant's conclusions from these studies were not verified by the FDA.

L337: ## **Study title/ number:** BLU-667: A 13-Week (Once Daily) Oral (Gavage) Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in Cynomolgus Monkeys / 00124768

L349: ## General toxicology; additional studies
L350: FDA generally agreed with the Applicant's summaries of the 28-day repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys, which evaluated higher dose levels than the 13-week toxicology studies. Similar target organs of toxicity were observed in the 28-day and 13-week toxicology studies in rats. In the 28-day rat toxicology study (# [REDACTED](b) (4)-124570), once daily oral administration of pralsetinib at the 75 mg/kg dose level (approximately 4.4 times the clinical AUC at the 400 mg dose) resulted in 38% and 42% mortality on Days 3-9 in male and female rats, respectively.
L351: Mineralization was present in multiple other tissues in rats including, but not limited, to the glandular stomach, aorta, heart, and spinal cord. Additional histologic findings in the heart included minimal to moderate hemorrhage and minimal to mild necrosis at the 75 mg/kg dose level; these findings were not present in recovery group animals. In the 28-day monkey toxicology study (#(b)(4)-124571), once daily oral administration of pralsetinib at the 40 mg/kg dose level (approximately 3.3 times the clinical AUC at the 400 mg dose) resulted in 100% mortality by Day 5, and one monkey dosed with 15 mg/kg pralsetinib (approximately 1.1 times the clinical AUC at the 400 mg dose) was found dead on Day 4. These deaths were due to extensive GI inflammation and/or ulceration leading to bacterial sepsis. Additional target organs in the 28-day monkey study that were not seen in the 13-week monkey study included the brain and spinal cord (gliosis), femur (physeal dysplasia), heart (hemorrhage and necrosis with bacteria at exposures approximately \ge 2.6 and \ge 1.1 times the clinical AUC at the 400 mg dose, respectively), bone marrow, liver (Kupffer cell brown pigment, hepatocellular vacuolation, subcapsular fibrosis), mandibular lymph node (decreased lymphoid cellularity, neutrophilic lymphadenitis), pancreas (acinar secretory depletion, hemorrhage), salivary gland (acinar secretory depletion, inflammation), and spleen (reduced lymphoid cellularity). In addition, treatment with \ge 15/7.5 mg/kg pralsetinib resulted in extensive histologic findings in the GI tract including mucosal atrophy, typhlitis, enteritis, colitis, intussception, and hemorrhage.
L352: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L353: ## The Applicant's Position:
L354: All genetic toxicology studies were conducted in accordance with GLP and currently accepted guidelines. Pralsetinib was not genotoxic in the in vitro and in vivo genotoxicity studies.
L355: ## **A GLP-compliant *Salmonella* *Escherichia coli*/Mammalian Microsome Reverse Mutation Assay (Ames Test) of Pralsetinib (Report (b)(4)-124573)**
L356: Pralsetinib was negative for mutagenic activity in the GLP-compliant in vitro 5-strain Ames bacterial reverse mutation assay, with and without metabolic activation (rat liver S9).
L357: ## **A GLP-compliant In Vitro Micronucleus Assay of Pralsetinib with TK6 Cells (Report 00124797)**
L358: Pralsetinib did not induce micronuclei in vitro in TK6 cells in the 4-hour treatment with and without metabolic activation and the 27-hour treatment without metabolic activation.
L359: ## **A GLP-compliant In Vivo Micronucleus Assay of Pralsetinib by Oral Gavage in Sprague Dawley Rats (Report 00124769)**
L360: Pralsetinib did not induce bone marrow micronuclei in vivo in Sprague Dawley rats (6 animals/sex/dose group) that were administered oral doses of pralsetinib of up to 300 mg/kg/day (1800 \text{ mg}/\text{m}^2/\text{day}) for 2 consecutive days.
L361: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L362: FDA confirmed the Applicant's presentation of the results of the submitted genotoxicity studies and agrees with the conclusions. Standard positive controls confirmed the sensitivity and validity of the assays.
L363: ## **5.5.3. Carcinogenicity**
L364: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L365: No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted as such studies are not required to support the registration of a drug intended for treatment of patients with advanced cancer, consistent with the ICH S9 guidance. Pralsetinib was not mutagenic in a bacterial reverse mutation assay (Ames test) and did not induce micronuclei in vitro in TK6 cells or in vivo in rats. There was no evidence of hyperplastic, preneoplastic, or neoplastic lesions in the 28-day and 13-week repeated-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys.
L366: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L367: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies are not needed to support the use of pralsetinib in the currently proposed indication per ICH S9.
L368: ## **5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology**
L369: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L370: Studies on reproductive function, early embryonic development, pre- and postnatal development, and effects in juvenile animals were not conducted in accordance with ICH S9 guideline. Embryofetal developmental toxicity was investigated in rats. Consistent with ICH S9, a confirmatory study in a second species was not conducted because teratogenicity and fetal lethality was observed in the rat embryofetal study.
L371: ## **A GLP-compliant Oral Gavage Enhanced Dose Range-Finding Study of the Effects of Pralsetinib on Embryofetal development with Toxicokinetics in Sprague Dawley Rats (Report 00124766)**
L372: In the GLP-compliant embryofetal development toxicology study, pralsetinib was administered at oral doses of 5, 10, 20, and 30 mg/kg/day to pregnant Sprague Dawley rats (10 animals/dose group) from Gestation Days 6 to 17; dosage levels were selected based on findings from the 28-day repeated-dose toxicology study in rats. Pralsetinib was tolerated in pregnant rats up to the highest tested dose of 30 mg/kg/day (180 mg/m2/day). Because of this, 30 mg/kg/day was the NOAEL for maternal toxicity. The NOAEL for embryofetal development could not be determined due to adverse effects on fetal morphology and/or intrauterine survival at all dosage levels. At 5 and 10 mg/kg/day (30 and 60 mg/m2/day), multiple visceral (kidney and ureter abnormalities) and skeletal (rib, costal cartilage, and vertebral anomalies) malformations were observed. The renal malformations were attributed to on-target inhibition of RET
L373: ## Pralsetinib
L374: signaling, as RET is known to be an essential component of renal organogenesis in utero. Mice homozygous for a mutation in the RET gene display renal agenesis or severe dysgenesis (Schuchardt et al, 1994). Ureteric bud growth from the Wolffian duct into the adjacent mesenchyme depends on sequential expression of the ligand glial cell line derived neurotrophic factor and its receptor, RET (Costantini and Shakya, 2006). Postimplantation losses of 100% were observed at dosage levels \ge 20 mg/kg/day (\ge 120 mg/m^2/day) and were attributed to pralsetinib's secondary pharmacologic effect of inhibiting VEGFR, an effect that has been observed with other anti-VEGF compounds due to their antiangiogenic effects (Patyna et al, 2008).
L375: ## The FDA's Assessment (all data presented by FDA):
L376: ## Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
L377: Fertility, early embryonic development, and juvenile animal studies were not conducted or needed to support the use of pralsetinib in the currently proposed indication (NSCLC) per ICH S9.
L378: ## Embryo-Fetal Development
L379: The Applicant did not submit GLP-compliant embryo-fetal development studies in two species. Rather, consistent with ICH S9, since a GLP-compliant embryofetal development study in rats demonstrated clear positive results at exposure multiples below the human exposure at the clinical dose of 400 mg, FDA agreed that this study was sufficient to convey the developmental risk to patients. FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the embryofetal study, and FDA's assessment is below.
L380: ## **Study title/ number: An Oral (Gavage) Enhanced Dose Range-Finding Study of the Effects of BLU-667 on Embryo/Fetal Development with Toxicokinetics in Sprague Dawley Rats / 00124766**
L381: ## **Key Study Findings**
L382: - Pralsetinib induced 100% embryo-fetal mortality due to post-implantation loss (early resorptions) at dose levels \ge 20 mg/kg (approximately 1.5 times the exposure based on AUC at the human dose of 400 mg) accompanied by reduced gravid uterine weight
- Pralsetinib was teratogenic at doses \ge 5 mg/kg (approximately 0.2 times the exposure based on AUC at the human dose of 400 mg)

Body Weights	There was a statistically significant \le 19\% decrease in mean body weight on GD 19-21 in rats dosed with 20 mg/kg pralsetinib compared to controls. L394: ## Prenatal and Postnatal Development
L395: No study or assessment conducted.
L396: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L397: ## The Applicant's Position:
L398: ## **Local Tolerance Studies**
L399: Stand-alone studies for local tolerance were not conducted and are not deemed necessary. Local tolerance of pralsetinib was characterized in GLP-compliant 28-day and 13-week repeated-dose (oral administration) toxicology studies in rats and monkeys.
L400: ## **In Vitro Phototoxicity Evaluation**
L401: Pralsetinib was classified as nonphototoxic in 1 non-GLP- and 1 GLP-compliant in vitro phototoxicity evaluation in 3T3 fibroblasts using the neutral uptake assay. No lesions in the epithelium of the skin or the eye were observed in 28-day or 13-week the repeated-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys.
L402: ## **Studies on Impurities**
L403: In silico assessment of drug substance impurities of pralsetinib, in vitro bacterial reverse mutation assay of drug substance impurities of pralsetinib, and in vivo GLP-compliant impurity qualification studies of drug substance impurities of pralsetinib in Sprague Dawley rats showed no specific concerns around impurity safety.
L404: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L405: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of local tolerance and conclusions regarding phototoxicity. In GLP-compliant phototoxicity Study #20143108, the Applicant evaluated the phototoxic potential of pralsetinib by measuring the relative reduction in viability of BALB/c 3T3 mouse fibroblasts exposed to pralsetinib and ultraviolet radiation compared to the viability of fibroblasts exposed to pralsetinib in the absence of ultraviolet radiation. Pralsetinib did not demonstrate phototoxic potential [Photoirritancy Factor (PIF) = 1] compared to the positive control promethazine (PIF = 75.82) under the conditions of this in vitro assay. The criterion for "phototoxic" is PIF &gt;5.
L406: ## **Studies on Impurities**
L407: FDA generally agreed with the Applicant's conclusion that the studies evaluating pralsetinib impurities (see below) did not demonstrate any specific safety concerns.
L408: ## **14-day Impurity Qualification Studies**
L409: The Applicant conducted two GLP-compliant 14-day oral repeat-dose toxicology studies in Sprague-Dawley rats evaluating the potential toxicity of four impurities. In Study #00124665, 10 rats/sex/group received 0, 10, or 20 mg/kg pralsetinib spiked with [REDACTED](b) (4) % [REDACTED](b) (4) (RRT [REDACTED](b) (4))
L410: and (b) (4) % (b) (4) (RRT (b) (4)) once daily for 14 days. In Study #00124746, 10 rats/sex/group received vehicle, 20 mg/kg pralsetinib spiked with (b) (4) % (b) (4) or 20 mg/kg pralsetinib spiked with (b) (4) % (b) (4) once daily for 14 days. There were no drug-related mortalities in either study. Overall, there were no new target organs of toxicity or new toxicologically relevant findings not seen in previous toxicology studies, suggesting the presence of these impurities did not change the toxicity profile of pralsetinib. Thus, impurities (b) (4) and (b) (4) were toxicologically qualified in rats. See Section 4 for further justification of the proposed specifications.
L411: ## **In Silico Genotoxicity Assessment of Potential Impurities (Studies #20182717 A-P)**
L412: The Applicant evaluated pralsetinib and 15 impurities for potential genotoxicity using DEREK NEXUS Version 6.01, a rules-based expert system, and SARAH NEXUS Version 3.0.0, a statistical based methodology for the prediction of mutagenicity. DEREK assessed alerts for carcinogenicity, genotoxicity, phototoxicity, and phospholipidosis and predicted bacterial mutagenicity. SARAH predicted in vitro mutagenicity.
L413: - Predicted negative by for mutagenicity by DEREK and SARAH: Pralsetinib, (b) (4) (b) (4)
- (b) (4) was predicted to be negative for mutagenicity by DEREK but was outside the training dataset domain of SARAH, so the overall assessment was inconclusive (see follow-up Ames assay below).
- (b) (4) and (b) (4) were predicted to be negative for mutagenicity by DEREK but were equivocal by SARAH, so the overall assessment was inconclusive (see follow-up Ames assay for (b) (4) below). Assessment with SARAH identified four negative supporting hypotheses for (b) (4) but they had a low confidence. (b) (4) is (b) (4) controlled (b) (4)
- (b) (4) was predicted to be negative for bacterial mutagenicity by DEREK and SARAH but there was an alert for mammalian chromosome damage in vitro in DEREK (see follow-up Ames assay below).
- No additional data or follow-up are needed
L414: ## In Vitro Bacterial Reverse Mutation Assays with Impurities
L415: The Applicant evaluated three pralsetinib impurities in non-GLP and GLP-compliant in vitro Ames assays. Standard positive and negative controls confirmed the sensitivity and validity of the assays. [REDACTED] (Study #00124872A, non-GLP; Study #00124873, GLP), [REDACTED] (Study #00124872B, non-GLP; Study #00124874, GLP), and [REDACTED] (Study #00124872C, non-GLP; Study #00124875, GLP) were not mutagenic at concentrations up to [REDACTED] \mug/plate using the *Salmonella typhimurium* strains TA1537, TA98, TA100, and TA1535 and *E. coli* strain WP2 *uvrA* in the presence or absence of S9.

L420: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L421: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L422: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L423: Pralsetinib is a tyrosine kinase inhibitor targeting oncogenic rearranged during transfection (RET) fusions and mutations. The proposed indication is for the treatment of adult patients with metastatic, *RET* fusion-positive, non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC). The proposed dosing regimen is 400 mg pralsetinib taken orally once daily (QD) on an empty stomach (i.e., at least 1 hour before and 2 hours after a meal).
L424: The registrational trial (Study BLU-667-1101; ARROW) is an open-label, single arm, Phase 1/2 trial which determined the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) in the dose-escalation phase and assessed the clinical efficacy and safety of pralsetinib in 179 patients with locally advanced or metastatic *RET* fusion-positive NSCLC in the dose-expansion phase. The overall response rate (ORR) (95% CI) was 70% (50%, 86%) in treatment naïve patients and 57% (46%, 68%) in patients previously treated with platinum chemotherapy.
L425: The clinical pharmacology package supporting this NDA included the following studies and data analyses: single- and repeat-dose pharmacokinetic (PK) studies of pralsetinib in healthy subjects and cancer patients, a mass balance study, a food effect study, drug-drug interaction (DDI) studies with itraconazole, rifampin, and esomeprazole, and a QTc prolongation assessment, exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety, and population PK (PopPK) analyses.
L426: The proposed dosing regimen for pralsetinib is based on efficacy and safety data from Study BLU-667-1101 and supported by E-R analyses for efficacy and safety. No large mean increase in QTc (&gt; 20 ms) was detected at the recommended dosing regimen. Administration of pralsetinib on an empty stomach is acceptable as food increased pralsetinib exposure by 122%. No clinically significant differences in the exposure of pralsetinib were observed when pralsetinib was coadministered with esomeprazole. Based on the observed 2.5-fold increase in pralsetinib exposure when pralsetinib was coadministered with itraconazole, coadministration of pralsetinib with strong CYP3A inhibitors should be avoided, and the current pralsetinib dose should be reduced by half when co-administered with combined (dual) P-gp and strong CYP3A inhibitors. Based on the observed 68% decrease in pralsetinib exposure when pralsetinib was co-administered with rifampin, coadministration of pralsetinib with strong CYP3A inducers should be avoided, and if this coadministration is unavoidable, the current pralsetinib dose should be doubled on Day 7 of coadministration.
L427: Exposure-safety analyses showed that increases in pralsetinib exposure correlate with time to Grade 3+ anemia or pneumonia. No exposure-efficacy relationship for Kaplan-Meier (KM) estimated progression-free survival (PFS) and the probability of ORR were observed. Intrinsic
L428: factors, including age, sex, mild and moderate renal impairment and mild hepatic impairment do not have clinically meaningful effects on the PK of pralsetinib.
L429: The following clinical pharmacology studies will be conducted under post-marketing requirements (PMRs): a dedicated hepatic impairment study in subjects with moderate and severe hepatic impairment; DDI studies for pralsetinib as a victim when co-administered with a P-gp inhibitor, a moderate CYP3A inhibitor, and a combined P-gp and moderate CYP3A inhibitor; DDI studies for pralsetinib as a perpetrator when co-administered with a cocktail enzyme substrates of CYP3A4/5, CYP2C8, and CYP2C9, and a cocktail transporter substrates of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE-1, and MATE-2K. A DDI study of pralsetinib as a victim when coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inducer will be conducted under post-marketing commitment (PMC).
L430: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the clinical pharmacology package supporting NDA 213721. This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. PMRs and PMCs are detailed in Table 10.
L431: **Table 10. Clinical Pharmacology Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments**

PMR	Determination of an appropriate safe pralsetinib dose for use in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.	Pralsetinib is primarily eliminated in the feces with minor excretion in the urine. In the clinical trials with pralsetinib, patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment were not enrolled. Based on the exposure-safety analysis, higher pralsetinib exposure was associated with higher risk of Grade \ge 3 anemia and pneumonia. A clinical study to evaluate the effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on the exposure of pralsetinib is required to inform necessary dosage adjustment in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
