L737: ## Dietary Restrictions/Instructions
L738: Pralsetinib was administered with a glass of water in a fasted state, with no food intake from 2 hours before until 1 hour after study drug administration.
L739: ## Concurrent Medications/Rescue Medications
L740: During the treatment period, the following medications, treatments, and procedures were prohibited: other anticancer therapies or investigational therapeutic agents; neutrophil growth factor within 14 days before Cycle 1 Day 1 and throughout Cycle 1 (unless patient experienced DLT of neutropenia); strong inhibitors and inducers of CYP3A4, strong dual P-gp and CYP3A4 inhibitors. Radiation therapy to target lesions or surgical removal of target lesions was considered indicative of PD.
L741: The following medication was not prohibited but was advised to be used with caution: sensitive substrates CYP2C9 substrates, strong CYP2D6 inhibitors, and strong CYP1A2 inhibitors.
L742: All other medications and treatments were permitted, including palliative and supportive care for disease-related symptoms.
L743: ## Treatment Compliance
L744: Patients returned all unused capsules (or empty bottles) on Day 1 of each treatment cycle or at the next scheduled visit. Compliance was assessed based on return of unused capsules (or empty bottles).
L745: ## Patient Completion, Discontinuation, or Withdrawal
L746: ## Completion/Discontinuation/Withdrawal from Study Treatment
L747: Patients could continue treatment until precluded by toxicity, noncompliance, closure of the study by Sponsor, or any reason for discontinuation as per protocol. Patients with PD could remain on treatment if in the opinion of the Investigator the patient has benefited from the pralsetinib therapy, and it was clearly in the best medical interest of the patient to remain on treatment.
L748: Patients had to discontinue from study treatment at any time for any of the following reasons: withdrawal of consent to continue study treatment or pregnancy. Patients could discontinue from study treatment for any of the following reasons: AE, disease progression, protocol deviation, Investigator's decision, or loss to follow-up.
L749: ## Completion/Discontinuation/Withdrawal from Study
L750: A patient was considered to have completed the study if he/she has completed all required visits shown in schedule of assessments (Table 15).
L751: Patients were withdrawn from study at any time for any of the following reasons: withdrawal of consent to continue study, death, or loss to follow-up.
L752: Before Protocol Amendment 9, patients who discontinued treatment were considered to have completed the study after a 30-day post-treatment follow-up visit or, for patients that had not yet experienced disease progression on study, after disease progression/initiation of new therapy. As Protocol Amendment 9 added follow-up for OS assessments, expected reasons for withdrawal from study were limited to withdrawal of consent, death, or loss to follow-up.
L753: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L754: FDA agrees with the description of Study BLU-667-1101 provided by the Applicant.
L755: ## **Study Endpoints**
L756: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L757: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint**
L758: *Phase 1:* Not applicable.
L759: ## *Phase 2:*
L760: The primary efficacy endpoint was the confirmed ORR based on RECIST v1.1 as assessed by BICR of local imaging. For BICR of local imaging, CT and magnetic resonance imaging scans were sent to a central radiology laboratory for assessment of response and progression by 2 board certified radiologists (if necessary, adjudication was performed by a third board certified radiologist) blinded to patient identification and starting dose. Central assessment provided an objective, unbiased, independent review of pertinent radiological data using uniform assessment rules to ensure a standard definition for all patients across this multicenter study.
L761: Disease response to treatment was assessed using radiographic assessments according to RECIST v1.1. These tests and evaluations are standard and appropriate for the evaluation of cancer patients and were based on appropriate response criteria that are widely accepted as valid and reliable measures of response to treatment.
L762: ## **Secondary Efficacy Endpoints**
L763: ## *Phase 1:*
L764: Secondary efficacy endpoints were
L765: - Confirmed ORR based on RECIST v1.1 as assessed by BICR of local imaging;
- Correlation of RET gene status in plasma and/or tumor tissue with ORR, CBR, DOR, PFS, OS, and DCR.
L766: ## *Phase 2:*
L767: Secondary efficacy endpoints were
L768: - DOR, CBR, DCR, PFS, and OS;
- Correlation of RET gene status in plasma and/or tumor tissue with ORR, CBR, DOR, PFS, OS, and DCR.
L769: ## **Exploratory Efficacy Endpoints**
L770: *Phase 1:* Not applicable.
L771: ## *Phase 2:*
L772: The exploratory efficacy endpoints were
L773: - Quality of life assessed by the EORTC QLQ-C30 questionnaire;
- CNS metastases activity analysis in RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients who had measurable (target) lesion(s) in CNS/brain at baseline.
L774: **FDA Clarification:** In a response to an FDA information request, the Applicant indicated that “CNS metastases activity analysis” was incorrectly listed here as exploratory. In that response, Blueprint stated that the analysis of CNS response was added as a secondary analysis in version 2 of the statistical analysis plan prior to database lock and analysis of CNS response has been added as a secondary endpoint in the most recent global protocol amendment (Protocol Amendment 13 dated 08 July 2020) submitted to IND (b) (4) on 15 July 2020.
L775: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L776: FDA considers the endpoints of CBR, DCR and “correlation of RET gene status in plasma and/or tumor tissue with ORR, CBR, DOR, PFS, OS, and DCR” to be exploratory in nature. Additionally, FDA considers the time to event endpoints of PFS and OS to be not interpretable in a single arm study, and any corresponding analyses were considered descriptive and were not confirmed by FDA review.
L777: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L778: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L779: The statistical analysis plan was finalized prior to the database lock.
L780: A data cutoff was established, and all analyses were performed based on the data up to this data cutoff date. Data from Phase 1 and 2 were pooled by starting dose level. Data from all centers were pooled.
L781: The primary efficacy analyses were based on BICR of local tumor imaging. Primary efficacy
L782: analyses were performed in the efficacy population subset of RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients exposed to \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib on or before 11 July 2019 who were treated at a pralsetinib starting dose of 400 mg QD (data cutoff of 18 November 2019).
L783: Efficacy analyses were also conducted for treatment groups of RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients receiving any prior systemic treatment, prior platinum treatment, and no prior platinum treatment (including prior non-platinum systemic treatment and no prior systemic treatment). Note that enrollment is still ongoing for Group 2 (NSCLC patients with no prior platinum treatment, including those who had not had any systemic treatment). According to the initial study design, Group 2 patients without prior systemic treatment had to be ineligible for standard platinum-based therapy before enrolling into the study. Note that prior selective RET-inhibitor-treated NSCLC patients (Group 6) were explored separately and were not included in the efficacy analyses.
L784: Secondary efficacy analyses were conducted in the efficacy population subset of RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients exposed to \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib on or before 11 July 2019 who were treated at a pralsetinib starting dose of 400 mg QD (data cutoff of 18 November 2019).
L785: In addition, efficacy was evaluated in the subpopulation of RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients with CNS metastases treated with pralsetinib at 400 mg QD. This subpopulation included all RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients who had measurable (target) lesion(s) in the brain at baseline according to RECIST v1.1 by BICR data and had not had any brain radiotherapy or radiosurgery within 2 months before pralsetinib treatment.
L786: Selected efficacy analyses were conducted for the subgroups of age (&lt; 65 years, \ge 65 years), sex, geographic region (US, Europe, Asia), race group (Asian, non-Asian), and RET genotype as determined by local testing and/or central analysis of ctDNA/tumor tissue (KIF5B, CCDC6, and “other”), and prior anticancer therapies (prior PD-1/PD-L1 or not, prior MKIs or not).
L787: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L788: Efficacy analysis was conducted based on two separate data cutoffs, the original data cutoff date of November 18, 2019, and one corresponding to the 90-day efficacy update, which had a data cutoff date of February 13, 2020. A further update for duration of response was provided based on data cutoff of May 22, 2020 for patients who had a confirmed response by the time of the 90-day efficacy update. The timing of the final analysis was not pre-specified in the protocol. The primary analysis of efficacy was conducted separately in patients who received prior platinum-based chemotherapy and in patients who were treatment-naïve. A supportive analysis was conducted in the population of patients with measurable disease at baseline (which included patients who had prior treatment with a therapy other than prior platinum-based chemotherapy).
L789: The final version of the statistical analysis plan, dated October 30, 2019, included assumptions for sample size calculation in the expansion phase for prior platinum therapy and the treatment-naïve cohorts. In cohort 1, which includes patients with RET-fusion positive NSCLC who were previously treated with platinum-based chemotherapy, assuming a null hypothesis that ORR=0.23 against an alternative hypothesis of ORR=0.5 with two-sided type I error rate of 0.05, a sample size of 80 will provide more than 95% power. Similarly, in cohort 2, which includes treatment-naïve patients with RET-fusion positive NSCLC, assuming a null hypothesis that ORR = 0.48 against an alternative hypothesis of ORR=0.61 at two-sided type I error rate of 0.05, a sample size of 170 will provide more than 90% power. There was no pre-defined plan for handling missing response assessments for ORR analysis.
L790: For a single arm study, FDA's efficacy analysis is based on ORR of adequate magnitude and a clinically meaningful duration of response. A statistically significant ORR compared to a historical rate may not necessarily be clinically meaningful.
L791: ## Protocol Amendments
L792: ## The Applicant's Description:
L793: The study protocol was amended 13 times during the study. Amendments 1, 2, 4.1, 7, and 9 were incorporated globally. These changes are not expected to have had an impact on the integrity of the trial or interpretation of the results. Key changes were as follows:
L794: ## Amendment 1 (dated 09 January 2017):
L795: - Inclusion criteria now include only patients with unresectable disease;
- Exclusion criteria now exclude patients with NSCLC with a targetable mutation in EGFR, ALK, or ROS1;
- Enrollment stopping rule was incorporated that terminated further enrollment to the study if there was an excess of permanent treatment discontinuations due to pralsetinib-related AEs;
- Strong inhibitors of CYP1A2, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4, as well as inducers of CYP3A4 and strong dual P-gp and CYP3A4 inhibitors were included in the list of prohibited medications;
L796: ## Amendment 2 (dated 02 February 2018)
L797: - Exclusion criterion 1 was revised to exclude NSCLC patients with known BRAF mutations.
L798: ## Amendment 4.1 (dated 25 July 2018)
L799: - Based on the favorable tolerability and encouraging efficacy observed in Phase 1, the sample size and overall enrollment for Phase 2 was increased to further evaluate the safety,
L800: - efficacy, and PK of pralsetinib in RET-altered patients treated at the MTD/RP2D;
- Group 6 was added in Phase 2 to assess pralsetinib safety and efficacy in patients previously treated with a selective RET inhibitor;
- The allowed ECOG performance status was changed to be 0 to 1;
- Quality of life assessments added.
L801: ## **Amendment 7 (dated 12 December 2018)**
L802: - Phase 2: Definitions of Groups 1 to 4 were adjusted to reflect previous treatment with approved standard of care agents. Group 5 was split into new Group 5 and new Group 7 to reflect that solid tumor patients with RET-fusions and RET-mutations may be distinct clinical and/or regulatory populations;
- Phase 2: Sample size increased to 80 patients in Group 1, 40 patients in Group 2, 60 patients in Group 3, and 40 patients in Group 5 to test the efficacy hypotheses for each group (increasing the study's total sample size to 360 patients);
- Phase 2: Safety Review Committee was added;
- Exclusion criteria added to exclude patients with Grade 2 or serum phosphorus at baseline or with clinically significant interstitial lung disease or interstitial pneumonitis.
L803: ## **Amendment 9 (dated 03 July 2019)**
L804: - The study Phase was updated from "1" to "1/2" to better describe the study design with the increased patient population and hypothesis testing;
- Phase 2 Group 2 sample size was increased to 200 patients due to the encouraging initial data in treatment-naïve RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients, to allow for enrollment of RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients with no prior treatment (increasing the study's total sample size to 527 patients);
- In Group 2, the requirement for NSCLC patients not to be candidates for standard platinum-based chemotherapy was removed. Patients were permitted to have prior platinum chemotherapy in the neoadjuvant and adjuvant setting if the last dose of platinum was \ge 4 months before the first dose of study drug;
- Stopping rules were updated to account for increased sample size;
- Dose modification for \ge Grade 3 pneumonitis was modified (patients had to be permanently discontinued from study treatment, unless the Investigator discussed with the Sponsor and believed the risk-benefit justified retreatment);
- Clarification was added that patients enrolled in Phase 1 and treated at the RP2D were pooled together with the appropriate Phase 2 groups for analyses;
- Statistics section was updated to align with the statistical analysis plan.
L805: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L806: FDA agrees with description of study amendments for Study BLU-667-1101 provided by the Applicant. In addition, FDA notes that post-progression follow-up was revised in Amendment 9
L807: to specify that patients with documented progression would be followed for survival, which will include subsequent antineoplastic therapy and survival status every 3 months until death, withdrawal of consent, or closure of the study by the Sponsor.

L824: Based on the review of the information submitted and the OSI inspection report on site # (b) (6)
L825: FDA agrees with the Applicant that the investigators involved in the BLU-667-1101 had no financial arrangements that would affect the outcome of the trial.

L859: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L860: ## Data:
L861: Of the 404 patients treated at all doses/schedules in the safety population, 121 patients (30.0%) had an important protocol deviation, most commonly related to study conduct/procedures (at any timepoint in 14.9% of patients and at Screening in 8.9% of patients). All other important protocol deviations occurred in &lt; 5.0% of patients. A total of 12 patients (3.0%) had a major protocol deviation based on medical review.
L862: Of the 132 NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD in the efficacy population, 45 patients (34.1%) had an important protocol deviation, most commonly related to study conduct/procedures (at any timepoint in 12.9% of patients, during Screening in 10.6% of patients, and relating to study restrictions/withdrawal criteria in 6.1% of patients). All other important protocol deviations occurred in &lt; 5.0% of patients. A total of 4 patients (3.0%) had a major protocol deviation based on medical review.
L863: ## The Applicant's Position:
L864: No deviations were identified that would affect the interpretation of results or conclusion of the primary analysis. Primary efficacy analysis was conducted in the efficacy population, with no patient removed from the analysis due to protocol deviations.
L865: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L866: A total of 522 instances of protocol deviations were reported in the efficacy population composed of patients with RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC with measurable disease, treated at 400 mg QD enrolled by date (N=114), of which 3 events were considered to be major by the Applicant, 55 were considered important deviations and 464 non-important deviations.
L867: The three major protocol deviations were: patient ID [REDACTED] (b) (6) had driver mutation in ctDNA (EGFR deletion that removes exons 2 &amp; 3 that code for part of the extracellular domain), patient ID [REDACTED] (b) (6) had history of brain metastases with baseline brain scan after 1st dose on study), and patient ID [REDACTED] (b) (6) had history of asymptomatic brain metastases with no baseline brain scan.
L868: The most common protocol deviations (388/519, 75%) were due to delayed or missing laboratory, ECG, serum for PK or imaging at the protocol specified time points.

L917: Source: Sponsor-provided ADaM datasets, adbl.xpt and addc.xpt
L918: **FDA Clarification:** FDA discovered a discrepancy in the dataset for the two patients in the prior platinum therapy subgroup reported as having stage IIIB NSCLC at screening. Blueprint acknowledged the discrepancy and provided additional information for these two patients. One patient had stage IIIB disease at screening with involvement of left hilar, subcarinal and right paratracheal lymph nodes, while the other had stage IV, M1a disease (metastatic) at screening with tumors in the left upper lobe and right upper lobe, as well as involvement of right hilar lymph nodes.
L919: **FDA Clarification:** The Applicant indicated that their calculation of patients with “CNS metastases” included patients who had medical history of metastases site in CNS/Brain from CRF data, or patients whose target or non-target lesion(s) location were in CNS/Brain, brainstem, cerebellum, frontoparietal, frontal lobe and frontotemporal at baseline according to RECIST v1.1 by BICR data. Hence according to the Applicant's calculation, 10 out of 27 (37%) patients had “CNS metastases” in the treatment-naïve subgroup.

L924: ## Treatment Compliance
L925: ## **Data:**
L926: Median relative dose intensity, which provides a measure of total dose administered relative to expected dose, was 91.9% across all NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. There were no clinically relevant differences among the prior treatment groups of NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L927: Most NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD in the efficacy population received concomitant medications during the study (99.2%). The most common concomitant medications were in the Anatomic Therapeutic Chemical class of Analgesics and Antipyretics (63.6%), followed by Opioids (53.0%), and Constipation Medications (52.3%). There were no clinically relevant differences in the types of concomitant medications among the prior treatment groups of NSCLC patients.
L928: Important protocol deviations related to dosing were reported in 2 patients (1.5%).
L929: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L930: Compliance was high in the efficacy population. There were only 2 cases of protocol deviations related to dosing that were considered important. This was considered unlikely to affect efficacy results.
L931: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L932: In Study BLU-667-1101, patients were dispensed the prescribed number of study drug bottles to allow for dosing for a full cycle, or until the next scheduled visit. Patients were instructed to return all unused drugs or the empty bottles on day 1 of each treatment cycle or the next scheduled visit. Compliance with the dosing regimen was assessed based on return of unused drug or empty bottles.
L933: Issues with investigational product handling/retention to assess compliance were reported in 6 out of 114 patients in the efficacy population. The patient ID and the reason for the deviation are listed in the following table.
L934: **Table 29 Treatment Compliance based on Investigation Product Handling/Retention (FDA Table)**
L935: Subject ID	Protocol Deviation: Investigational Product Handling/Retention
(b) (6)	Compliance at Cycle 1 was not assessed as Vial (b) (6) was not returned by patient and was not at pharmacy. SC contacted patient who does not know where the vial is, and CRA confirmed it hasn't been returned to pharmacy.
Referencing to Subject's diary for Cycles 1 and 2, there was discrepancy of 4 extra capsules returned. Subject clarified he did not miss any doses.
Cycle 2 Day 2: 31 capsules were returned instead of the expected 52 capsules. Subject claims that the 21 capsules were accidentally dropped and disposed of while she was admitted in the ward and confirmed there was no overdosing. Entries recorded in dosing diary were noted to be correct
Cycle 1: 48 capsules were returned instead of the expected 44 capsules, 4 extra capsules returned denoting 95% compliance. Subject claims that no doses were missed.
Patient expired on 24 Feb 2019 during Cycle 2. But family members refused request to return cycle 2 IP administration diary.

L937: Based on the available information, compliance with the dose regimen was high and the deviations reported are unlikely to affect the overall efficacy results of the trial.
L938: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L939: ## Data:
L940: ORR in patients with RET-fusion positive NSCLC treated at 400 mg QD overall was 56.8% (95% CI: 47.9, 65.4): 8 patients (6.1%) with CR and 67 patients (50.8%) with PR (Table 30). Beyond that, 41 patients (31.1%) had SD and 10 patients (7.6%) had PD. ORR results were similar among NSCLC patients irrespective of prior treatment: ORR was 54.4% (95% CI: 44.3, 64.2) in NSCLC patients with any prior systemic treatment, 53.3% (95% CI: 42.6, 63.7) in NSCLC patients with prior platinum treatment, and 65.5% (95% CI: 45.7, 82.1) in NSCLC patients with no prior systemic treatment.
L941: RET genotype was determined by local testing and/or central analysis of ctDNA.
L942: ORR in NSCLC patients with KIF5B genotype (N=94) was 59.6% (95% CI: 49.0, 69.6): 4 patients (4.3%) with CR and 52 patients (55.3%) with PR. Beyond that, 28 patients (29.8%) had SD and 7 patients (7.4%) had PD.
L943: ORR in NSCLC patients with CCDC6 genotype (N=22) was 63.6% (95% CI: 40.7, 82.8): 3 patients
L944: (13.6%) with CR and 11 patients (50.0%) with PR. Beyond that, 5 patients (22.7%) had SD and 1 patient (4.5%) had PD.
L945: Although ORR was numerically higher in patients with CCDC6 compared to patients with KIF5B, the CIs overlapped.

L952: ## The Applicant's Position:
L953: Based on ORR analysis pralsetinib was highly active in RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, across all lines of prior therapy. It must be noted that according to the initial study design, patients without prior systemic treatment had to be ineligible for standard platinum-based therapy before enrolling into the study. As a result, these patients had more advanced cancer and a poorer prognosis. Despite the poor prognosis of these patients, a high ORR was achieved.

L956: analysis for this application.

L969: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
L970: ## **Data:**
L971: ## Duration of Response
L972: Median DOR in the 75 NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD with a confirmed response has not been reached at the time of data cutoff (95% CI: 11.3, -), with 61 patients (81.3%) still in response and 14 patients (18.7%) with progression (Table 32). Median (range) time to first response was 1.81 months (1.0 to 9.1 months). Estimated DOR rates were 86.0% at 6 months (95% CI: 77.0, 95.1) and 74.6% at 9 months (95% CI: 61.3, 87.8) overall. A total of 41 patients (54.7%) had DOR \ge 6 months overall (64.3% in the any prior treatment group, 61.2% in the prior platinum group, and 26.3% in the no prior treatment group), 22 patients (29.3%) had DOR \ge 9 months overall (35.7% in the any prior treatment group, 30.6% in the prior platinum group, and 10.5% in the no prior treatment group), and 6 patients (8.0%) had DOR of \ge 12 months overall (10.7% in the any prior treatment group, 10.2% in the prior platinum group, and no patients in the no prior treatment group).
L973: Of the 34 patients with estimated DOR rate of &lt; 6 months, 20 patients were still in response (10 patients in any prior treatment group, 9 patients in the prior platinum group, and 10 patients in the no prior treatment group).
L974: DOR results were similar among NSCLC patients irrespective of prior treatment: Median DOR has not been reached at the time of data cutoff (95% CI: 11.3, -) in NSCLC patients with any prior systemic treatment, with 46 patients (82.1%) still in response and 10 patients (17.9%) with progression. Estimated DOR rates were 85.8% (95% CI: 75.9, 95.6) at 6 months and 78.0% (95% CI: 64.3, 91.6) at 9 months in this group. Median DOR in NSCLC patients with prior platinum treatment was also not reached (95% CI: 11.3, -), with 39 patients (79.6%) still in response and 10 patients (20.4%) with progression. Estimated DOR rates were 83.7% (95% CI: 72.6, 94.8) at 6 months and 73.9% (95% CI: 57.8, 90.0) at 9 months in this group. Median DOR in NSCLC patients with no prior treatment is currently 7.4 months (95% CI: 6.3, -), although it is noted that this estimate is preliminary as 15 patients (78.9%) remain in response and only 4 patients (21.1%) have experienced progression. Estimated DOR rates were 87.5% (95% CI: 64.6, 100.0) at 6 months and 46.7% (95% CI: 2.4, 91.0) at 9 months in this group.
L975: It must be noted that, according to the initial study design, patients without prior systemic treatment had to be ineligible for standard platinum-based therapy before enrolling into the study. As a result, these initial patients had more advanced cancer and a poor prognosis. With Amendment 9, more typical, first-line, treatment-naïve patients were enrolled. Thus, most of the NSCLC patients who had no prior treatment were enrolled later in the study and their response duration is not as long as that possible in other groups at this data cut.
L976: RET genotype was determined by local testing and/or central analysis of ctDNA.
L977: Median DOR in NSCLC patients with KIF5B genotype (N=56) was not reached (95% CI: 8.8, -), with 42 patients (75.0%) still in response and 14 patients (25.0%) with progression. Estimated
L978: DOR rates were 81.2% at 6 months (95% CI: 69.5, 93.0) and 65.6% at 9 months (95% CI: 48.5, 82.7) in this subgroup.
L979: Median DOR in NSCLC patients with CCDC6 genotype (N=14) has not been reached at the time data cutoff, with all patients (100.0%) still in response.

L1002: ## Clinical Benefit Rate
L1003: CBR in this patient population was 68.2%, with 90 patients having confirmed CR, PR, or SD for \ge 4 cycles (Table 30). CBR results were similar irrespective of prior treatment. In patients with no prior treatment, CBR was 65.5% (95% CI: 45.7, 82.1).
L1004: ## Disease Control Rate
L1005: DCR in NSCLC patients was 87.9% (95% CI: 81.1, 92.9), with 116 patients having CR, PR, or SD (Table 34). There were no clinically relevant differences in DCR among the prior treatment groups of NSCLC patients. In patients with no prior treatment, DCR was 79.3% (95% CI: 60.3, 92.0).

L1026: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1027: The observed DOR, PFS, CBR, DCR, and OS in RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD support the meaningful clinically important activity of pralsetinib in this patient population with high unmet medical need.
L1028: It must be noted that according to the initial study design, patients without prior systemic treatment had to be ineligible for standard platinum-based therapy before enrolling into the study. As a result, these initial patients had more advanced cancer and a poorer prognosis contributing to shorter apparent DOR, PFS, and OS. However, despite the fact that these were poor prognosis patients due to original eligibility criteria, clinically meaningful results were
L1029: achieved for DOR, PFS, and OS.
L1030: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1031: Since FDA considers time-to-event endpoints not interpretable in a single arm study, statistical analyses of PFS and OS under Study BLU-667-1101 were considered descriptive and were not verified. Furthermore, FDA considers the analyses related to CBR and DCR to be exploratory and did not verify the results.
L1032: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or Exploratory Clinical Outcome Assessment (Patient-reported Outcomes) Endpoints**
L1033: ## **Data:**
L1034: ## **Quality of Life**
L1035: EORTC QLQ-C30 were collected for patients enrolled on or after Protocol Amendment 7 during Phase 2, where all patients' starting dose was 400 mg QD. Overall, the median (range) global health status score in NSCLC patients was 83.0 (75 to 100) of 100 possible points at the last assessment (Week 48) in the EORTC QLQ-C30, with minimal changes (\le 16 points) from baseline observed at all timepoints over a duration of 48 weeks, except a change of 25.0 points at Week 40 in the prior platinum treatment group and for 1 patient in the no prior treatment group at the last assessment. High mean and median scores have been reported for physical, role, emotional, cognitive, and social functioning indicating a high level of functioning, with minimal changes from baseline observed at all timepoints (mostly &lt; 10 points change from baseline). At baseline, patients reported low levels of clinical symptoms, which remained low with little changes throughout all timepoints for fatigue, nausea/vomiting, dyspnea, appetite loss, and diarrhea. From baseline to last assessment, there was a tendency toward less pain and insomnia (change of \le 16 points) and toward more constipation (consistent with what was seen for AE reporting). Low mean and median scores were observed for the scale financial difficulties, with no relevant changes from baseline observed at all timepoints.
L1036: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1037: Patient-reported overall quality of life assessments showed that patients' quality of life was maintained throughout treatment. Quality of life in respective functioning subscores was consistent with AE reporting and as expected for this patient population. There were no clinically relevant differences in quality of life results among the prior treatment groups of NSCLC patients.
L1038: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1039: There are many factors that make patient-reported outcome data from Study BLU-667-1101 difficult to interpret. This study was open-label, so patient knowledge of treatment assignment
L1040: may induce bias in the completion of PRO instruments. There was no pre-specified analysis plan for the patient-reported outcomes, so there was no control of Type I error or threshold for a clinically meaningful difference for these analyses. Finally, since there are no comparative analyses for this non-randomized study, it is difficult to attribute any observed within-patient differences of PROs to the experimental therapy, as the results cannot be put into context for this patient population or disease without a control arm.
L1041: ## Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
L1042: ## Data:
L1043: ## Pralsetinib Against Intracranial Metastases
L1044: Analysis of best overall response in patients with CNS metastases was performed in the response-evaluable population, which included CNS patients from the efficacy population who had a measurable (target) disease per RECIST v1.1 at baseline, \ge 1 evaluable postbaseline disease response assessment performed as per BICR, and no major protocol violations (incomplete baseline imaging; patient in Groups 1, 2, 5, 6, or 7 without evidence of RET mutation or fusion; patient had another known primary driver alteration other than RET identified by central ctDNA/tumor assay).
L1045: Among the 9 response-evaluable patients treated at 400 mg QD with measurable intracranial target lesions at baseline, the RECIST v1.1 CNS ORR was 55.6% (95% CI: 21.2, 86.3) (3 patients with CR, 2 patients with PR) (Table 37). Beyond that, 4 patients (44.4%) had SD.
L1046: In the efficacy population, among the NSCLC patients without CNS involvement prior to study entry, none of the 77 treated at 400 mg QD developed new CNS involvement during the study according to central review data. Among the 8 NSCLC patients without CNS involvement who received lower pralsetinib starting doses in the efficacy population, 1 patient developed new CNS involvement. This patient was treated at 100 mg QD for the first 5.5 months on study, then escalated to 300 mg QD before developing new CNS disease at 7.3 months on study.

L1056: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1057: Pralsetinib penetrates the blood-brain barrier and is efficacious in brain metastases.
L1058: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1059: Among the 87 patients with RET fusion-positive NSCLC who had received prior platinum-based chemotherapy, 8 had measurable CNS metastases at baseline as assessed by BICR. No patients received radiation therapy to the brain within 2 months prior to study entry. Responses in intracranial lesions, as assessed by the BIRC per RECIST 1.1, were observed in 4 of these 8 patients, including 2 patients with a CNS complete response; 75% of responders had a DOR of ≥6 months. One patient who had received prior PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor and had measurable CNS metastases at baseline achieved a complete response as assessed by BICR per RECIST 1.1.
L1060: ## **Subgroup Analyses**
L1061: ## Data:
L1062: There were no statistically relevant differences in pralsetinib efficacy in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD based on time to response, DOR, and ORR for the subgroups of age (&lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years), sex (females, males), geographic region (US, Europe, Asia), or race (Asian, non-Asian). There were also no statistically relevant differences in ORR by prior anticancer therapy (prior PD-1/PD-L1 treatment or not, prior MKI treatment or not).
L1063: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1064: No apparent outliers were observed in subgroup analyses.

L1066: The following table provides FDA's exploratory ORR analysis by age, sex, and race separately in the prior platinum therapy and treatment naïve subgroups. These subgroup analyses are considered exploratory; however, there were no obvious outliers were observed in these analyses.

L1071: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L1072: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1073: The clinical and statistical review teams conclude that the Applicant provided substantial evidence of effectiveness of pralsetinib in the following populations:
L1074: - adult patients with treatment-naïve RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC and
- adult patients with RET-fusion positive metastatic NSCLC who have previously received platinum-based chemotherapy.
L1075: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1076: This section is not applicable because the application is supported by one trial.
L1077: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1078: ## Data:
L1079: Based on the efficacy analyses at the time of data cutoff, pralsetinib is highly active in RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, across all lines of prior therapy.
L1080: The key efficacy results for the efficacy population were:
L1081: - ORR based on central radiology review by RECIST v1.1 was 56.8% (95% CI: 47.9, 65.4) in the overall NSCLC population (N=132);
L1082: ## Pralsetinib
L1083: - NSCLC patients with prior platinum treatment (N=92): ORR was 53.3% (95% CI: 42.6, 63.7) with 49 patients having achieved best confirmed response of CR or PR;
- NSCLC patients with any prior systemic treatment (N=103): ORR was 54.4% (95% CI: 44.3, 64.2) with 56 patients having achieved best confirmed response of CR or PR;
- NSCLC patients with no prior systemic treatment (N=29): ORR was 65.5% (95% CI: 45.7, 82.1) with 19 patients having achieved best confirmed response of CR or PR. It must be noted that, according to the initial study design, patients without prior systemic treatment had to be ineligible for standard platinum-based therapy before enrolling into the study. As a result, these initial patients had more advanced cancer and a poor prognosis. With Amendment 9, more typical, first-line, treatment-naïve patients were enrolled. Despite the poor prognosis of these patients, a high ORR was achieved;
- ORR in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD with prior PD-1/PD-L1 treatment (N=48) was 58.3% (95% CI: 43.2, 72.4). ORR results were similar in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD with no prior PD-1/PD-L1 treatment (N=84): ORR was 56.0% (95% CI: 44.7, 66.8).
- Median DOR has not been reached at the time of data cutoff, with 81.3% of patients still in response. Estimated DOR rates were 86.0% at 6 months and 74.6% at 9 months. Median (range) time to first response was 1.81 (1.0 to 9.1) months;
- DCR was 87.9% (95% CI: 81.1, 92.9), with 116 patients having CR, PR, or SD;
- CBR was 68.2%, with 90 patients having CR, PR, or SD for \ge 16 weeks if 28 days in a cycle;
- Median PFS in the overall NSCLC population was 12.7 months (95% CI: 9.1, -), with an estimated PFS rate of 69.1% at 6 months, 60.5% at 9 months, and 51.6% at 12 months;
- A total of 95 patients (72.0%) remained alive as of the data cutoff date. KM estimate for median OS could not be determined at a median follow-up of 10.5 months. The estimated survival rate was 85.7% at 6 months, 78.8% at 9 months, and 73.8% at 12 months;
- Pralsetinib penetrates the blood brain barrier and is efficacious in brain metastases: Among the 9 response-evaluable patients treated at 400 mg QD with measurable intracranial lesions at baseline, the RECIST CNS ORR was 55.6% (95% CI: 21.2, 86.3) (3 patients with CR, 2 patients with PR). No patients without history of CNS involvement and receiving a starting dose of 400 mg QD developed new CNS metastases on study;
- Quality of life assessments showed that patients' quality of life was maintained during treatment with pralsetinib;
- Overall, there were no clinically significant differences in efficacy among the prior treatment groups of NSCLC patients.
L1084: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1085: The efficacy results from NSCLC patients in Study BLU-667-1101 show that pralsetinib treatment at a dose of 400 mg in a QD schedule offers a substantial improvement over current standard treatments for patients with RET-altered advanced NSCLC. The ORR for confirmed CR or PR (primary endpoint) was 56.8% (95% CI: 47.9, 65.4) and was similar irrespective of prior treatment: ORR was 54.4% with any prior systemic treatment, 53.3% with prior platinum treatment, and 65.5% with no prior systemic treatment. This is more than double the ORR that was achieved with the current standard of care for post platinum treatment (9% to 14%) (Borghaei et al, 2015; Brahmer et al, 2015; Fehrenbacher et al, 2018; Garon et al, 2014; Hanna et al, 2004) and also higher than the 46% (95% CI: 41, 50) for first-line patients treated with atezolizumab plus protein-bound paclitaxel plus carboplatin (Atezolizumab USPI, Dec 2019) and the 47.6% (95% CI: 42.6, 52.5) achieved for first-line patients treated with the triple combination of pembrolizumab plus pemetrexed plus platinum-based chemotherapy (Gandhi et al, 2018). Moreover, the higher ORR in first-line NSCLC patients in this study was achieved despite the fact that these were poor prognosis patients due to the original eligibility criteria.
L1086: Furthermore, the DOR rate, DCR, PFS, and estimated 12-month OS rate were all better with pralsetinib treatment than what has been reported with current standard treatments in the nonsquamous population, and these efficacy outcomes were seen regardless of whether patients had any prior systemic treatment or no prior systemic treatment (Table 39).

L1095: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1096: Based on the review of clinical data from Study BLU-667-1101 (ARROW), the clinical and statistical review teams conclude that the Applicant provided substantial evidence of effectiveness of pralsetinib in patients with RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC adequate to support accelerated approval.
L1097: Data from Study BLU-667-1101 demonstrated that pralsetinib administered to patients with RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC who have previously received platinum-based chemotherapy (N=87) results in an ORR per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by a BIRC of 57% (95% CI 46, 68). Tumor responses were durable, with 80% having responses lasting ≥6 months. In the population of patients with treatment-naïve RET fusion-positive NSCLC (N=27), the ORR was 70% (95% CI 50, 86), with 58% having responses lasting ≥6 months.
L1098: ORR of sufficient magnitude in conjunction with a clinically meaningful duration of response has been accepted by the Office of Oncology to be likely to predict clinical benefit in patients with rare NSCLC mutations. The reviewer notes that given the small sample size, in particular for the population of patients with treatment-naïve RET fusion-positive NSCLC (N=27), the magnitude of ORR and duration of response with pralsetinib is associated with some uncertainty. Additional data from the ongoing Study BLU-667-1101 (ARROW) are warranted to verify benefit in this population. Blueprint has also initiated a randomized, controlled trial of pralsetinib compared to platinum-based chemotherapy with or without pembrolizumab as first-line treatment for patients with metastatic RET fusion-positive NSCLC (Study BLUE-667-2303; AcceleRET Lung), and submission of data from this trial will be requested as a post-marketing commitment to provide additional information regarding outcomes with pralsetinib in the treatment-naïve patient population.
L1099: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1100: ## Data:
L1101: The primary evidence for the safety of pralsetinib in patients with RET-positive locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC comes from the pivotal Study BLU-667-1101. The study included a Phase 1 dose escalation part to determine the MTD and RP2D of pralsetinib, followed by a Phase 2 dose expansion part to assess the clinical efficacy of pralsetinib in specific tumor types and treatment settings (measured primarily by ORR) and further define the safety and tolerability at the MTD/RP2D.
L1102: Included are safety data from patients enrolled in Study BLU-667-1101 up to 18 November 2019, the data cutoff date for this marketing authorization application. A total of 404 patients have received \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib with any indication (NSCLC, (b) (4) (i.e., consistent with the safety population), including 62 patients enrolled in the Phase 1 and 342 patients in the Phase 2. Of those, 354 patients received pralsetinib at a starting dose of 400 mg QD in either Phase 1 or Phase 2, regardless of tumor type or mutation status. A total of 179 patients with NSCLC were treated at 400 mg QD. Any SAEs from 19 November through 18 December 2019 are reported, for completeness, as late-breaking safety information.
L1103: The assessment of safety is supported by the data from 5 clinical pharmacology studies in healthy subjects.
L1104: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1105: Data from the pivotal Study BLU-667-1101 allowed for a comprehensive and adequate assessment of the safety profile of pralsetinib and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in adult patients with RET-positive locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC at a dose of 400 mg QD. The 404 patients included in the safety population includes 354 patients treated at 400 mg QD and 179 NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, which are considered adequate for the detection and characterization of common AEs, and to provide guidance on management of AEs.
L1106: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1107: The FDA agrees that the available safety data allow for an adequate assessment of the safety profile of pralsetinib. The safety population used to inform Section 5 of labeling is all patients who started pralsetinib at 400 mg once daily, the proposed dosing regimen for this NDA. Data from additional patients who received a starting dose of 400 mg once daily was available after the 90-day safety update (DCO February 13, 2020). Thus, 438 patients were included in the safety analysis population for FDA's review.
L1108: ## **8.2.1. Safety Review Approach**
L1109: ## **Data:**
L1110: The data presented here focus on all patients treated with pralsetinib at 400 mg QD (N=354) as
L1111: of the NDA data cutoff date (18Nov2019). Summary of safety data from patients with RET-positive locally advanced or metastatic NSCLC treated at 400 mg QD (N=179) is also provided. Patients included were those exposed to \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib at 400 mg on the initial dose prescribed on Day 1 in either phase of Study BLU-667-1101.
L1112: Of the 354 patients treated at 400 mg QD, the most common AEs (reported in &gt; 20\%) were AST increased, constipation, anaemia, diarrhoea, ALT increased, hypertension, pyrexia and fatigue. The events of pneumonia, pneumonitis, and tumor lysis syndrome were identified as AESIs during the development of pralsetinib, based on the clinical experience taking into consideration multiple factors such as incidence, severity, relationship to study drug, medical impact, and clinical outcome of the AEs.
L1113: Among the 438 patients treated at 400 mg QD as of Day 90 Safety Update (data cutoff date 13Feb2020), the profile of common AEs (in \ge 20\% of patients) was consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA. The only additional PTs that met the \ge 20\% incidence threshold through the update period that had not met this threshold in the initial NDA were white blood cell count decreased (increase from 16.1% to 20.3%) and neutropenia (19.8% to 20.1%). As in the initial NDA, most common events in all patients treated at 400 mg QD (in \ge 20\% of patients) by PTs included AST increased, constipation, anaemia, hypertension, ALT increased, diarrhoea, fatigue, WBC count decreased, neutropenia and pyrexia.
L1114: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1115: Safety data from clinical studies showed that the common AEs of pralsetinib among all patients treated at 400 mg QD were primarily AST increased, constipation, anaemia, diarrhoea, ALT increased, and hypertension, with AESIs of pneumonia, pneumonitis, and tumor lysis syndrome identified as part of the comprehensive safety review during the development of pralsetinib.
L1116: The FDA's Assessment: FDA's assessment of the most common adverse reactions in patients with RET fusion-positive NSCLC and a discussion of safety issues considered for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of the pralsetinib label are presented in the sections below.
L1117: ## 8.2.2. **Review of the Safety Database**
L1118: ## Overall Exposure
L1119: ## Data:
L1120: The safety population from Study BLU-667-1101 was defined as all patients who were exposed to \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib, regardless of starting dose or cancer diagnosis as of 18 November 2019. Patients were analyzed based on the initial dose prescribed on Day 1, regardless of study phase. A total of 404 patients received \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib, 354 patients received pralsetinib at a starting dose of 400 mg QD, 197 NSCLC patients received pralsetinib at all doses/schedules, and 179 NSCLC patients received pralsetinib at a starting dose of
L1121: 400 mg QD.
L1122: Of the 354 patients treated at 400 mg QD, median (range) treatment duration was 5.52 (0.1, 22.3) months, 5.65 (0.2 to 30.8) months in NSCLC patients treated at all doses/schedules, and 5.16 (0.2 to 19.8) months in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD (Table 40). There were no clinically meaningful differences in exposure among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, and the overall safety population based on prior systemic treatment, prior platinum trial treatment, or prior non-platinum treatment.
L1123: Among the 438 patients treated at 400 mg QD as of Day 90 Safety Update (data cutoff date 13Feb2020), median duration of treatment was 5.24 months (range &lt; 1 to 25.1 months). There were no meaningful changes as compared to what was reported in the initial NDA.

L1141: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:

L1162: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1163: ## Data:
L1164: The safety database for this submission included 404 patients who received \ge 1 dose of pralsetinib from the pivotal Study BLU-667-1101. Among these 404 patients, 354 patients were treated at 400 mg QD and 179 had NSCLC and were treated with 400 mg QD. Among the 354 patients, the median (range) treatment duration was 5.52 (0.1, 22.3) months (Table 40). Among the 179 patients, the median (range) treatment duration was 5.16 (0.2 to 19.8) months.
L1165: As of the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), the median (range) treatment duration was 5.24 (&lt;1, 25.1) in patients treated at 400 mg QD which was consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1166: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1167: The safety database was considered adequate to evaluate the safety of pralsetinib in the intended population at the target dose.
L1168: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1169: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment of the adequacy of the safety database.
L1170: ## **8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments**
L1171: ## **Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality**
L1172: ## **Data:**
L1173: All safety evaluations conducted during Study BLU-667-1101, including monitoring for AEs, physical examinations, vital sign measurements, clinical laboratory tests, and ECGs are considered standard (i.e., widely used and generally recognized as reliable, accurate, and relevant).
L1174: Study BLU-667-1101 was conducted according to procedures that incorporate the ethical principles of GCP. To ensure compliance with these procedures and to assess the adequacy of quality control procedures, audits of study documentation, Investigator centers, and the CSR were performed by an external auditor that operated independently of the study monitors.
L1175: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1176: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site audits; data in the electronic case report forms and AE databases were consistent.
L1177: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1178: The data submitted was organized and adequate to perform a complete review of the safety of pralsetinib. Overall, FDA agrees that there were no significant data quality or reporting issues identified during the review of this NDA. FDA did issue several information requests during the review cycle to obtain clarification and additional information regarding safety data included in the NDA.
L1179: ## **Categorization of Adverse Events**
L1180: ## **Data:**
L1181: Each patient was carefully monitored for the development of any AEs throughout the study from the time of signing informed consent to 30 days after the last dose. In addition, SAEs that were assessed as related to study treatment that occurred &gt; 30 days post-treatment were reported.
L1182: All AEs (serious and nonserious) spontaneously reported by the patient and/or in response to
L1183: an open question from study personnel or revealed by observation, physical examination, or other diagnostic procedures were recorded in the appropriate section of the electronic case report form.
L1184: Adverse events were evaluated based on TEAEs, which were defined as AEs that occurred during or after administration of the first dose of pralsetinib through 30 days after the last dose, any event that was considered pralsetinib-related regardless of start date, or any event that was present at baseline but worsened in intensity, or was subsequently considered pralsetinib-related by the Investigator.
L1185: All AEs were coded using MedDRA version 19.1. The severity was graded per the NCI CTCAE version 4.03.
L1186: Relationship to study drug administration was determined by the Investigator according to the following criteria:
L1187: - Not related: Exposure to the study treatment did not occur, or the occurrence of the AE was not reasonably related in time, or the AE was clearly caused by something other than pralsetinib;
- Related: The AE did not meet the criteria for “not related”.
L1188: The safety of treatment with pralsetinib was evaluated on the basis of the following:
L1189: - Incidence, type, severity, and causal relationship of AEs to study treatment;
- Incidence of deaths, SAEs, and other clinically significant AEs (including AEs leading to interruption of study drug, dose reduction of study drug, and permanent discontinuation of study drug);
- Incidence, type, and severity of AEs by age, race, geographic region, and sex;
- Incidence of AESIs (pneumonia, pneumonitis, and tumor lysis syndrome), where categories (pneumonia and pneumonitis) were defined by groups of selected and relevant PTs;
- Incidence of other grouped AEs (neutropenia and thrombocytopenia);
- Exploratory time to event analyses including time to onset, time to improvement, and time to resolution for AESI terms and neutropenia and hypertension.
L1190: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1191: The approach to the analyses of AEs was appropriate in evaluating the safety data.
L1192: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1193: For purposes of the FDA review of safety, incidences of adverse events were analyzed without consideration of relatedness, given the safety data supporting this NDA is from a non-randomized, non-comparative study.
L1194: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1195: ## Data:
L1196: The tables of study procedures and scheduled assessments in the protocol for Study BLU-667-1101 specified the timepoints at which laboratory evaluations were conducted.
L1197: Clinical laboratory values were graded according to NCI CTCAE version 4.03 for applicable parameters except creatinine, which was graded according to NCI CTCAE version 5.0 to correct the grading error for this specific laboratory test used in NCI CTCAE version 4.03.
L1198: Overall summary and changes from baseline were presented for hematology, serum chemistry (including coagulation), and thyroid test laboratory values. Shift tables of laboratory data from baseline to worst grade during treatment were presented. Urinalysis was presented in listings only.
L1199: For the overall safety population at all doses and NSCLC patients at 400 mg QD, boxplots were presented by visit for selected laboratory tests including ALT, AST, alkaline phosphatase, total bilirubin, phosphate, sodium, calcium, corrected calcium, potassium, hemoglobin, leukocytes, neutrophils (absolute), lymphocytes (absolute), platelets, and thyroid-stimulating hormone.
L1200: Results outside predefined normal ranges and NCI CTCAE grade were flagged in the data listings.
L1201: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1202: The laboratory evaluation (laboratory tests and timepoints) as described in the protocols was reasonable and adequate to monitor the safety of the population, disease, and indication being investigated.
L1203: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1204: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement. Please refer to Table 15: Schedule of Assessments for schedule of assessments. For hyperphosphatemia, FDA requested additional analyses and re-grading of hyperphosphatemia utilizing NCI CTCAE version 5.0.
L1205: ## 8.2.4. **Safety Results**
L1206: ## Deaths
L1207: ## Data:
L1208: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), death due to a SAE were reported in 29 patients (8.2%), including 1 patient due to a related SAE (pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia). These SAEs included pneumonia cytomegaloviral, pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, dyspnoea,
L1209: respiratory failure, acute myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, sepsis, urinary tract infection, asthenia, and haemorrhage intracranial (in 1 patient each), medullary thyroid cancer (2 patients), pneumonia (3 patients), disease progression (14 patients).
L1210: In NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, deaths due to SAEs were reported in 19 patients, and none of these deaths was assessed as related to pralsetinib by the Investigator. Specifically, these SAEs were pneumonia (3 patients), pneumonia cytomegaloviral, dyspnoea, cerebrovascular accident, sepsis, urinary tract infection, asthenia, and haemorrhage intracranial (1 patient each), and disease progression (9 patients).
L1211: Since the data cutoff of 18 November 2019, 5 additional deaths were reported through the late-breaking data cutoff date of 18 December 2019. One event of pneumonitis was considered related to pralsetinib treatment. The patient had SAEs of febrile neutropenia (Grade 3), acute myocardial infarction (Grade 3), sepsis (Grade 3), cerebral infarction (Grade 4), and pneumonitis (Grade 5), which led to the patient's death.
L1212: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), no relevant increase (\ge 1\%) in incidence was noted for treatment-related deaths. Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, 40 patients (9.1%) died during the study due to AEs and 3 additional deaths were reported due to a related AE (pneumonitis, and death [2 patients]). The event of pneumonitis was included in the late breaking section of the initial NDA. Twenty patients (4.6%) died due to disease progression.
L1213: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1214: In the safety population of patients treated at 400 mg QD as well as in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, the SAEs leading to death were mostly associated with patients' underlying disease and/or complications. Most deaths were due to disease progression. This is consistent with data from the overall safety population of patients treated at all doses/schedules.
L1215: ## The FDA's Assessment:

L1218: FDA confirmed the information and reviewed all death narratives summarized in the table above, as well as narratives for deaths attributed to disease progression. The Applicant categorized deaths as due to disease progression, unknown, or adverse event. The Applicant further categorized death due to adverse events as related to study drug or not related. Deaths that occurred more than 30 days post-treatment were not captured in the table above but
L1219: were reviewed and no clinically significant safety signals were identified.
L1220: Patient narratives for deaths due to adverse events of pneumonia, pneumonitis or sepsis were reviewed in detail and are summarized below. In Section 6 of USPI, which will present data for the NSCLC safety population (n=220), pneumonia (n=3) and sepsis (n=2) will be listed as fatal adverse reactions occurring in &gt;1 patient (see details below).
L1221: ## Pneumonia
L1222: - Patient (b) (6): A 66 year old female patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to adrenals, bone, CNS/brain and liver. Patient was hospitalized due to pulmonary embolism; a few days into her hospital stay she was diagnosed with pneumonia cytomegaloviral infection. CT revealed patchy areas of increased density with a ground glass pattern and a tendency toward confluence and diffuse affection of both lung fields. Patient was treated with ganciclovir 5 mg/kg IV every 12 hours. However, after 7 days of treatment patient died due to pneumonia cytomegaloviral.
L1223: *Reviewer comments: Although the patient's death was due to pneumonia cytomegaloviral and it cannot be ruled out that pralsetinib did not contribute to this event, the patient was previously taking dexamethasone prior to and while on study, which may have pre-disposed her to an infection.*
L1224: - Patient (b) (6): 81 year old male patient diagnosed with MTC was metastatic to lung and lymph nodes was diagnosed with pneumonitis and hospitalized. CT scan showed ground glass opacities and areas of consolidation predominantly in the perihilar region. The next day patient was diagnosed with pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia. Treatment included sulfamethoxazole and trimethoprim and was admitted to the ICU. CT angiogram was performed and showed no evidence of pulmonary embolism and clear intensification of interstitial lung disease. 10 days later patient died in the ICU due to respiratory insufficiency secondary to pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia.
L1225: *Reviewer comment: Applicant attributed cause of death, pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, to pralsetinib, and FDA agrees based on the information provided.*
L1226: - Patient (b) (6): 59 year old female patient with PTC metastatic to bone, CNS/brain, liver, and lung was hospitalized due to pneumonia. Physical exam showed crepitation in right lung and x-ray showed opacity consistent with right middle lobe pneumonia. Labs showed neutropenia and severe thrombocytopenia and leucopenia. Treatment was discontinued, however the following morning patient experienced cardiac arrest and died due to pneumonia.
L1227: ## Pralsetinib
L1228: *Reviewer comment: Patient was neutropenic; neutropenia is consistent with the known toxicity of pralsetinib and may have predisposed her to an infection. It is possible that pneumonia was related to treatment with pralsetinib, but complication of patient's disease is possible.*
L1229: - Patient (b)(6): 56 year old male patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to bone, CNS/brain, liver and lung with left plural effusion experienced pneumonia 4 different times while on treatment. Patient experienced grade 3 pneumonia which required hospitalization. CT scan confirmed pneumonia. The event resolved after appropriate antibiotic treatment. However, 12 days following the report of resolution, patient experienced hemoptyxis and aggravation of dyspnea. CT scan revealed pneumonia in the lung and increased irregular ground glass opacity in both lungs and consolidation in both lungs. A week following event patient experienced Grade 5 pneumonia and died the same day.
L1230: *Reviewer comment: There is a possibility that pneumonia events were related to pralsetinib; however, definite attribution is confounded due to risk factors as well as patient's poor condition.*
L1231: - Patient (b)(6): 77 year old male patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to CNS/brain, lung, lymph nodes, enterocoelia, and pleura experienced hypophosphatemia which required hospitalization. The day after the patient experienced Grade 4 pneumonia. Patient was treated with cefoperazone sodium/sulbactam sodium, cilastatin sodium/imipenem and carbazochrome. However, the patient's condition worsened the following day and an X-ray showed right lung cancer and bilateral pneumonia. Patient died the following day due to lung infection.
L1232: *Reviewer comment: Patient's pneumonia may be attributed to complication of disease; however, pralsetinib's role in patient's pneumonia cannot be ruled out.*
L1233: ## Sepsis
L1234: - Patient (b)(6): 59 year old female patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to bone, CNS/brain, liver, lung and lymph nodes experienced sepsis which required hospitalization. Patient had bacterial peritonitis post cholecystectomy. Labs showed neutrophils, leukocytes, platelets and hemoglobin to be abnormal. Klebsiella pneumonia was detected in the blood culture. Patient was treated with meropenem, vancomycin, and linezolid. Patient died due to worsening of septic condition.
L1235: *Reviewer comments: Pneumonia may have contributed to sepsis. While assessment of attribution is difficult due to confounding factors of bacterial peritonitis and patient's prolonged and complicated hospital stay, contribution of pralsetinib cannot be ruled out.*
L1236: ## Pralsetinib
L1237: - Patient (b)(6): 54 year old female patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to adrenals, lymph nodes, pericardial mass and pleura experienced a SAE of urinary tract infection. Patient admitted to hospital and blood and urine cultures revealed Escherichia coli. CT scan showed an increase in the amount of loculated effusion in the left lower hemothorax. Patient received piperacillin sodium, levofloxacin for urinary tract infection. However, ongoing urinary tract infection led to sepsis and patient died following admission into hospital.
L1238: *Reviewer comment: Patient's laboratory values showed no myelosuppression while on study drug. UTI/sepsis could possibly be related to study drug as patient did not have many risk factors to predispose her to UTI/sepsis.*
L1239: ## Pneumonitis
L1240: - Patient (b)(6): 60 year old male patient diagnosed with MTC metastatic to bone and lung experienced Grade 3 pneumonitis and was admitted to the hospital. Ongoing conditions included poor appetite, productive cough and waist pain. CT angiogram was done, and pneumonitis was confirmed. Treatment with study drug was interrupted. Blood cultures showed no infectious causes. Treatment was re-started at a lower dose. Patient was discharged from the hospital and 11 days later patient experienced respiratory failure and died at home.
L1241: *Reviewer comment: Patient re-started pralsetinib at a lower dose despite event of Grade 3 pneumonitis; it's possible that pneumonitis recurred or worsened after discharge due to re-starting pralsetinib.*
L1242: - Patient (b)(6): 57 year old female patient with **NSCLC** metastatic to the liver; prior anti-cancer therapies included carboplatin, pemetrexed, and selpercatinib. On Study Day 30 this patient experienced febrile neutropenia which required hospitalization. Patient's stay was complicated by MI and sepsis. Her hospital stay was then prolonged due to pneumonitis. CT scan showed extensive ground glass densities and interlobular septal thickening in both lungs, which was consistent with inflammatory process rather than infectious process. All infectious work up was negative. Dexamethasone was started. She continued her hospital stay but patient experienced cerebral infarction and pneumonitis was considered resolved. However, the following day patient experienced pneumonitis which led to patient's death on the same day.
L1243: *Reviewer comment: Xrays and cultures did not indicate pneumonia and Applicant noted pneumonitis was related to study drug. FDA agrees with this assessment.*
L1244: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1245: ## **Data:**
L1246: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), serious adverse events were reported in 157 patients (44.1%), of whom 58 (16.4%) had related SAEs. In patients with NSCLC treated at 400 mg QD, SAEs were reported for 91 patients (50.8%), of whom 36 (20.1%) had related SAEs. The most common SAEs in all patients treated at 400 mg QD (occurring in \ge 2 patients) were pneumonia (8.2%), disease progression (5.1%), pneumonitis (4.0%), sepsis (2.5%), urinary tract infection (2.8%), and pyrexia (2.5%), which were also the most common SAEs in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1247: The most common related SAEs (occurring in \ge 2 patients in all patients treated at 400 mg QD) were pneumonitis (3.1%), pneumonia (2.0%), and anaemia, hypertension, neutropenia, pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, sepsis, and thrombocytopenia (&lt; 1% each) (Table 47). Of the 13 patients with related SAEs of pneumonitis, 11 patients were treated with 400 mg QD as their starting dose.
L1248: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, Grade 3 related SAEs were reported by 39 patients (11.0%), Grade 4 related SAEs were reported in 10 patients (2.8%), and Grade 5 related SAEs were reported in 1 patient (&lt;1%). Similar percentages of patients reported each grade in the NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.

L1254: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, no notable differences in the profile of SAEs were seen compared to what was reported in the initial NDA. Serious adverse events were reported in 195 patients (44.5%), of whom 77 (17.6%) had related SAEs. The most common SAEs in all patients treated at 400 mg QD (occurring in ≥ 2 patients) were pneumonia (7.5 %), disease progression (5.7%), pneumonitis (4.6%), urinary tract infection (3.0%), sepsis (2.5%), and pyrexia (2.3%).
L1255: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1256: The most common SAEs in all patients treated at 400 mg QD (occurring in ≥ 2 patients) were pneumonia (8.2%), disease progression (5.1%), pneumonitis (4.0%), sepsis (2.5%), urinary tract infection (2.8%), and pyrexia (2.5%), which were also the most common SAEs in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1257: The most common related SAEs (reported in \ge 2 patients) were pneumonitis (3.1%) and pneumonia (2.0%). Pneumonitis is a known risk associated with the use of TKIs as well as other cancer therapies such as immune checkpoint inhibitors. The risk of pneumonia increases in patients with lung cancer.
L1258: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1259: FDA confirmed the results in the above table based on the Applicant's assessment of relatedness. FDA completed additional analyses on serious events based on the 90 day update and regardless of is the assessment of relatedness to pralsetinib. In order to fully characterize the differences between the safety populations of patients with NSCLC and all patients treated at 400 mg daily, all SAE which occurred in \ge 1\% of patients are presented. The incidence of SAE was similar in the subgroup of patients with NSCLC and the all patients treated at 400 mg QD safety population.

L1262: ## **Analysis of Potential Off-Target Effects (FDA Review and Assessment)**
L1263: FDA reviewed trends in SAEs and evaluated SAEs based on non-clinical data of potential off target effects associated with inhibition of VEGF receptor JAK1/2 and FGFR pathways.
L1264: Hemorrhages, pneumonia/pneumonitis, tumor lysis syndrome (refer to 8.2.5), GI perforations, UTI/sepsis/infections, hepatic events, hyperphosphatemia, wound healing, cardiac events,
L1265: thromboembolic events and vision disorders were further examined. FDA requested additional analyses of some of these events from the Applicant. An overview of these analyses, which are based on the safety population of 438 patients treated with pralsetinib 400 mg QD, is provided below.
L1266: FDA conducted additional assessments/analyses for potential adverse reactions related to inhibition of the VEGF pathway, FGFR pathway and JAK1/2 pathway. Regarding potential adverse reactions associated with the VEGF pathway, hypertension is discussed separately in Section 8.2.5, since the Applicant included this in the evaluation of “Significant Adverse Events”.
L1267: ## VEGF pathway
L1268: ## *Hemorrhagic Events*
L1269: Hemorrhagic events (any grade) were reported in 16% of patients as identified by the SMQ hemorrhage, with 2.5% patients experienced Grade 3 events or higher. Dose modifications occurred as a result of these events. One fatal hemorrhagic event was reported. The Applicant noted that the fatal event of intracranial hemorrhage occurred in a 71 year old male patient with advanced NSCLC and metastasis to the brain, liver and mediastinal lymph nodes. He was receiving aspirin and clopidogrel at that time. He fell and imaging demonstrated a small hemorrhagic left cerebellar metastatic lesion; the fatal event of intracranial hemorrhage occurred about 3 weeks after initiation of pralsetinib. Although there are confounding factors of CNS metastatic disease as well as dual anti-platelet therapy, there is a possibility that pralsetinib may have contributed to the patient's outcome.
L1270: Narratives were reviewed for all serious Grade 3 or higher events of hemorrhage were reviewed, and all SAEs were associated with potential confounders/risk factors such as concurrent anticoagulant treatment, biopsy procedure, accidental fall, and/or hemorrhagic brain metastasis. However, based on non-clinical data inhibition of the VEGF pathway may occur with pralsetinib. Thus, it's possible pralsetinib may contribute to risk of experiencing a hemorrhagic event. Given this and the potential seriousness of these events, Hemorrhagic Events will be included in Section 5 of the USPI.

L1273: ## *Hepatotoxicity*
L1274: FDA requested additional information for Grade 3 or higher serious hepatic events. The Applicant provided an analysis of hepatic events. There were 10 SAEs of Grade 3 or higher hepatic events in 7 patients identified (see table below). Of these 7 patients, 5 patients required dose modifications. No fatalities attributed to hepatic events were identified and no events met Hy's law criteria. Hepatic laboratory abnormalities of increased AST (69% Grade 1-4) and increased ALT (46% Grade 1-4) were captured in assessment of laboratory shift tables (Table 59 Select Lab Abnormalities (\ge20%) Worsening from Baseline in Patient who received 400 mg of GAVRETO in ARROW). The median time to first onset for increased AST was 15 days and for increased ALT was 22 days. Based on this analysis, health care providers need to be aware of recommended dose modifications for patients experiencing hepatic laboratory abnormalities, and Hepatotoxicity will be included in Section 5 of the USPI.

L1277: ## *GI perforations/fistulas*
L1278: FDA requested an analysis of cases of GI perforations/fistulas. The Applicant's analysis revealed 11 events of GI perforation/fistula in 8 patients. Confounders or risk factors of prior medical history were present in 6 out of 8 patients as described in the table below. Based on the currently available information, FDA did not deem it necessary to place GI perforations/fistulas in Section 5 of the USPI at this time; however, based on the potential for pralsetinib to impact the VEGF pathway and the fact that the limited available data is from a single arm study, a PMR was requested to further characterize the incidence, clinical presentation, management and outcome of GI perforations/fistulas.

L1281: ## *Increased Risk of Impaired Wound of Healing*
L1282: FDA requested additional information regarding impaired wound healing. The Applicant noted there were only 4 events of impaired wound healing in 4 patients. One had a Grade 3 event and the other 3 patients had a Grade 1 event. No events resulted in pralsetinib dose modifications. Based on review of preclinical data for pralsetinib and clinical data for multiple drug products that inhibit the VEGF pathway, FDA determined this may be a class effect and Section 5 of the USPI will include Risk of Impaired Wound Healing with instructions to withhold pralsetinib for at least 2 weeks following surgery and until adequate wound healing.
L1283: ## *Cardiac failure*
L1284: SMQ analyses for cardiac failure and cardiomyopathy were performed by the Applicant at the request of FDA.
L1285: The SMQ analysis for cardiac failure did not generate any \ge Grade 3 events with the preferred terms peripheral edema, peripheral swelling, cardiac failure, cardiac failure congestive, edema, orthopenea, and pulmonary edema. Although the search generated any grade events in 15% of patients, the vast majority of these events were for the preferred term peripheral edema. The SMQ for cardiomyopathy generated 22 events that were \ge Grade 3, which included 8 events of dyspnea and 7 events of syncope. This included one event of chest pain, three events of ascites, one event of orthostatic hypotension and three events of mental status change. Events of dyspnea and syncope were reviewed, but given confounding factors it was considered unlikely that pralsetinib contributed to the occurrence of these events of cardiac dysfunction.
L1286: ## *Thromboembolic events*
L1287: SMQ analyses for thromboembolic events were performed by the Applicant at the request of the FDA.
L1288: The SMQ analyses for embolic and thrombotic events, venous revealed 11 \ge Grade 3 events with the preferred terms pulmonary embolism, deep vein thrombosis, portal vein thrombosis, thrombophlebitis superficial, and superior vena cava syndrome. Nine events of pulmonary embolism occurred. These narratives were reviewed; most patients had NSCLC or had metastases to the lungs as well as disease progression. The incidence is not higher than expected in a population of patients with advanced malignancy. The SMQ for embolic and thrombotic events, arterial generated 3 \ge Grade 3 events which included preferred terms acute myocardial infarction and myocardial infarction. There was one fatal event due to myocardial infarction in a patient with multiple risk factors for cardiovascular disease, such as age, hypertension, and diabetes. These analyses do not suggest a safety signal for pralsetinib for thromboembolic events.
L1289: ## FGFR Pathway
L1290: ## *Hyperphosphatemia*
L1291: FDA requested the Applicant to provide additional analyses on patients with hyperphosphatemia. At FDA's request, the Applicant re-graded hyperphosphatemia using CTCAE v5.0 for Grade 1-4. There were no serious cases of hyperphosphatemia, no patients had phosphate values of &gt;9 mg/dL and phosphate elevations were generally moderate.
L1292: FDA initially considered adding this event to Section 5 of the USPI as dose modifications and
L1293: management guidelines, including administration of phosphate binders, for hyperphosphatemia were included in the amended protocol for ARROW. It was later revealed by the Applicant that the Korean health authority, based on non-clinical findings, requested this addition of management guidelines for hyperphosphatemia. Of note, the Applicant's ongoing randomized clinical trial, Study BLU-667-2303, does not include management guidelines for hyperphosphatemia. Additionally, as indicated by the Applicant's table below, approximately 50% of patients with abnormal phosphate values were not treated with phosphate binders. Information submitted by the Applicant demonstrated that patients who were not treated with phosphate binders did not have worsening hyperphosphatemia over time and administration of phosphate binders did not appear to decrease the duration of hyperphosphatemia.
L1294: FDA added hyperphosphatemia to Section 6 of the USPI as a potentially clinically significant adverse reaction of pralsetinib.
