L1303: ## Vision Disorders
L1304: An SMQ analyses of vision disorders was provided by the Applicant at the request of the FDA. The results revealed two \ge Grade 3 events, one event of keratitis and one of cataract. These analyses do not suggest a safety signal for pralsetinib for vision disorders.
L1305: ## Jak1/2 Pathway
L1306: ## *Increased risk of infections/Myelosuppression*
L1307: FDA requested the Applicant to perform additional analyses to assess the potential association of underlying neutropenia to sepsis events. The Applicant's analysis revealed 13 serious adverse events of sepsis; 7 events were Grade 3, 5 events were Grade 4 and 1 event was Grade 5. Nine events resolved, 2 events resolved with sequelae and 1 event was ongoing; 1 event had a fatal outcome. In all events, baseline absolute neutrophil counts were within normal range. Six events were occurred in association with decreased neutrophil counts.
L1308: The primary infection was UTI in 5 events and pneumonia in 2 events. Confounders and risk factors were present in 11 of the 13 patients including history of neutropenia, ongoing urinary tract infection and disease progression. As infections are commonly reported in cancer patients and in ARROW these events were not consistently associated with neutropenia, FDA determined this did not warrant inclusion in the USPI. Sepsis, pneumonia and UTI are included in the list of the most frequent SAE in Section 6 of the USPI.

L1316: ## Data:
L1317: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), adverse events that were the primary or contributing reasons for permanent treatment discontinuation (including “disease progression”

L1321: reported as an AE term and AEs that represented signs/symptoms of disease progression) were reported in 58 patients (16.4%) and in 35 (19.6%) NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. The most common events (occurring in &gt; 1% of patients) were similar in all patients treated at 400 mg QD and in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. In all patients treated at 400 mg QD, these were disease progression (3.4%), and pneumonia (1.4%) and pneumonitis (1.1%). L1322: Through Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, no notable differences in incidence of adverse events leading to permanent treatment discontinuations were seen compared to what was reported in the initial NDA. The most common AEs leading to permanent treatment discontinuation (in ≥ 1% of patients) in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were disease progression (2.7%), pneumonitis (1.4%), and pneumonia (1.4%).
L1323: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1324: The most common AEs leading to permanent treatment discontinuation in patients treated at 400 mg QD were disease progression (per the reported AE PT) (3.4%), and pneumonia (1.4%) and pneumonitis (1.1%).

L1331: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1332: Data:
L1333: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, adverse events leading to dose modifications were reported by 232 patients (65.5%) and 117 (65.4%) of NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. In both populations most of the dose modifications (approximately 60%) were dose interruptions while only 1 of 3 were dose reductions (33% to 34%).
L1334: The most common adverse events leading to dose interruption (reported in &gt; 3% of patients) were similar in all patients treated at 400 mg QD and in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. In all patients treated at 400 mg QD these were neutropenia (9.3%), hypertension (7.3%), pneumonitis (7.1%), anaemia and pneumonia (5.9 % each), AST increased (4.5%), blood creatine phosphokinase increased (3.7 %), and pyrexia and diarrhoea (3.4 % each). The only differences in the events reported by NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD compared with the all patients treated at 400 mg QD was that vomiting was also reported in &gt; 3% of patients (3.4%) while blood creatine phosphokinase increased was reported in &lt; 3% of patients.
L1335: The most common adverse events leading to dose reduction (reported in ≥ 2% of patients) were also similar in all patients treated at 400 mg QD and in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. In all patients treated at 400 mg QD these were neutropenia (7.3 %), anaemia (5.6%), pneumonitis (4.0%), and hypertension (3.1%), and neutrophil count decreased (2.0%). The same pattern of adverse events resulted in dose reductions in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, but included pneumonia and AST increased (2.2% each).
L1336: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, no notable differences in incidence of adverse events leading to permanent treatment discontinuations were seen compared to what was reported in the initial NDA. Adverse events leading to treatment interruption were reported in 267 patients (61.0%) and dose reductions in 121 patients (34.2%). The most common AEs leading to dose interruption and dose reduction were also consistent with what were reported in the initial NDA.
L1337: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1338: The most common AEs leading to dose interruption (reported in &gt; 3% of patients) in patients treated at 400 mg QD were neutropenia (9.3%), hypertension (7.3%), pneumonitis (7.1%), anaemia and pneumonia (5.9 % each), AST increased (4.5%), blood creatine phosphokinase increased (3.7 %), and pyrexia and diarrhoea (3.4 % each) and these were similar in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1339: The most common AEs leading to dose reduction (reported in &gt; 2% of patients) in patients treated at 400 mg QD were neutropenia (7.3 %), anaemia (5.6%), pneumonitis (4.0%), and hypertension (3.1%), and neutrophil count decreased (2.0%) and these were similar in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1340: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1341: FDA agrees with the Applicant's analyses of dose interruptions and dose reductions. Updated data for dose interruptions and dose reductions based on the 90 day safety update are presented in the tables below. Among the 220 patients with NSCLC treated with pralsetinib 400 mg QD, 36% required dose reductions and 60% required dose interruptions. No major differences were noted between the NSCLC population the all patients treated at 400 mg QD safety population.

L1349: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1350: ## Data:
L1351: In addition to the AESIs of pneumonia, pneumonitis, and tumor lysis syndrome, which are discussed in detail in Section 8.2.5, the grouped adverse events of neutropenia and thrombocytopenia, and hypertension (not grouped) were selected for further analysis.
L1352: ## Pralsetinib
L1353: Neutropenia and thrombocytopenia are adverse events commonly observed with TKIs such as cabozantinib and vandetanib (Lyseng-Williamson, 2018; Morabito et al, 2010). In addition, nonclinical studies have demonstrated that pralsetinib inhibits JAK2, which has been shown to reduce red blood cell, neutrophil, and/or platelet counts (Huang and Luo, 2019; Pardanani and Tefferi, 2014; van Vollenhoven et al, 2012; Verstovsek et al, 2010).
L1354: Based on nonclinical evidence, pralsetinib doses \ge 25 mg/kg resulted in increased blood pressure in rats, which was attributed to VEGFR inhibition. Based on clinical evidence, hypertension occurred with increased frequency in the clinical development program. Adverse events such as hypertension and plausible mechanism of action due to VEGFR inhibition were also seen in other TKIs.
L1355: ## **Neutropenia**
L1356: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), 115 patients (33%) reported events of neutropenia or neutrophil count decreased (referred to as grouped neutropenia events). These included 37 patients (11%) reported neutropenia events of \ge Grade 3 in severity and 15 patients (4%) reported neutrophil count decreased events \ge Grade 3 in severity. One hundred five of the 354 patients (30%) treated at 400 mg QD reported grouped neutropenia events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1357: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=438), 158 patients (36.1%) reported grouped neutropenia events, including 64 (14.6%) patients with Grade 3 events, and 10 (2.3%) experienced Grade 4 events; no patient experienced Grade 5 events. A total of 146 patients (33.3%) reported grouped neutropenia events that were assessed as related to pralsetinib.
L1358: Overall, neutropenia events included in the Day 90 Safety Update are consistent with those in the initial NDA.
L1359: ## **Thrombocytopenia**
L1360: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, 50 patients (14.1%) reported events of thrombocytopenia or platelet count decreased (referred to as grouped thrombocytopenia events). A total of 5 patients (1.4%) had Grade 3, and 3 patients (&lt; 1%) had Grade 4 thrombocytopenia AEs. None of the thrombocytopenia AEs was Grade 5. A total of 2 patients (&lt; 1%) had Grade 3, and 1 had (&lt; 1%) Grade 4 platelet count decreased AEs. None of the platelet count decreased AEs were Grade 5. Forty-five of the 354 patients (13%) treated at 400 mg QD reported grouped thrombocytopenia events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1361: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=438), 66 (15.1%) reported grouped thrombocytopenia events,
L1362: ## Pralsetinib
L1363: including 9 (2%) patients with Grade 3 events and 5 (1.1%) with Grade 4 events. No patient experienced Grade 5 events. A total of 58 patients (13.2%) reported grouped thrombocytopenia events that were assessed as related to pralsetinib.
L1364: Overall, thrombocytopenia events included in the Day 90 Safety Update are consistent with those in the initial NDA.
L1365: ## ***Hypertension***
L1366: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), 99 patients (28%) reported events of hypertension. A total of 14 patients (4%) patients reported Grade 1 events, 36 patients (10%) reported Grade 2 events, 49 patients (14%) reported Grade 3 events. No patients experienced Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. Seventy-two of the 354 patients (20%) treated at 400 mg QD reported hypertension events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1367: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=438), 128 (29.2%) reported events of hypertension, including 20 patients (4.6%) with Grade 1 events, 45 (10.3%) with Grade 2 events, and 63 (14.4%) with Grade 3 events. No patient experienced Grade 4 or Grade 5 events. A total of 96 (21.9%) patients reported hypertension events that were assessed as related to pralsetinib.
L1368: Overall, hypertension events included in the Day 90 Safety Update are consistent with those in the initial NDA.
L1369: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1370: Based on the biological plausibility and events reported as summarized above, a causal association to pralsetinib appears possible. These events were manageable by dose modification and routine medical care.
L1371: The available safety database was adequate to identify and characterize these events.
L1372: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1373: FDA does not have any comments regarding thrombocytopenia but has the following comments for neutropenia and hypertension.
L1374: Neutropenia: FDA requested additional information on adverse event of increased risk of infection and requested Applicant to provide analyses of any cases of sepsis associated with neutropenia and to provide a summary of febrile neutropenia cases (see 8.2.4 for details). Applicant did not provide additional analysis of febrile neutropenia, however There were 4 patients who experienced febrile neutropenia (Grade 3); no patient had pralsetinib permanently discontinued for febrile neutropenia.
L1375: ## Pralsetinib
L1376: Hypertension: FDA did not request additional analyses of hypertension. Of note, no Grade 4 or 5 events of hypertension occurred with pralsetinib; however, hypertension occurred in 29% of patients, and 14% experienced Grade 3 events. Pralsetinib was interrupted for hypertension in 7% of patients and 3.2% had dose reduction of pralsetinib for hypertension. One patient discontinued treatment due to hypertension. Hypertension will be included in Section 5 of the USPI as the awareness of this adverse reaction and the need to implement appropriate management strategies are important in this population in order to mitigate any adverse outcomes.
L1377: ## **Treatment Emergent Adverse Events**
L1378: ## Data:
L1379: ## **Overview of Adverse Events**
L1380: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354), 339 patients (95.8%) experienced AEs, 320 (90.4%) experienced AEs related to pralsetinib as assessed by the Investigators, 155 (43.8%) experienced ≥ Grade 3 related AEs, 156 (44.1%) experienced SAEs, and 58 (16.4%) experienced related SAEs (Table 57). Treatment with pralsetinib was discontinued due to AEs as the primary or contributing reason in 41 patients (11.6%) treated at 400 mg QD (17 patients (4.8%) due to a related AE).
L1381: In general, the safety profile of patients treated at 400 mg QD was similar to that of NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. Of the 179 RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD in the safety population (in either Phase 1 or Phase 2 of the study), 169 (94.4%) experienced any AEs, 161 (89.9%) experienced AEs related to pralsetinib, 78 (43.6%) experienced ≥ Grade 3 related AEs, 91 (50.8%) experienced SAEs, and 36 (20.1%) experienced related SAEs (Table 57). Treatment with pralsetinib was discontinued due to AEs in 35 patients (2 due to a related AE).

L1388: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), AE profile remains consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1389: ## **Common Adverse Events**
L1390: The most common AEs (reported in &gt; 20% of patients) in patients treated at 400 mg QD were AST increased (38.7%), followed by constipation (34.2%), anaemia (31.9%), diarrhoea (27.7%), ALT increased (27.1%), hypertension (28.0%), and fatigue (21.5%). There were no clinically meaningful differences between patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354) and all patients (N=404) in the safety population.
L1391: In NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD, the most common AEs were the same as those in all patients treated at 400 mg QD. There were no clinically meaningful differences between NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=179) and all NSCLC patients treated at all doses/schedules (N=197).
L1392: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), the profile of the most common AEs remains consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1393: ## **Grade ≥ 3 Adverse Events**
L1394: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, an AE of Grade ≥ 3 was reported by 232 patients (65.5%) and by 114 (63.7%) NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. The most common AEs of Grade ≥ 3 (reported in ≥ 10% patients) in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were hypertension (13.8%), anaemia (12.1%), and neutropenia (10.5%) and were the same in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD. There were no clinically meaningful differences between all patients treated at 400 mg QD and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1395: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), the profile of the severity of AEs remains consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1396: ## ***Related Adverse Events***
L1397: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, 320 patients (90.4%) experienced at least 1 related AE. In the NSCLC safety population treated at 400 mg, 161 patients (89.9%) experienced at least 1 related AE.
L1398: The most common related AEs (reported in &gt; 20% of patients) in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were AST increased (31.4%), ALT increased (21.5%), anaemia (21.8%), and hypertension (20.3%). There were no clinically meaningful differences between all patients treated at 400 mg QD and NSCLC patients at 400 mg QD.
L1399: A total of 155 patients (43.8%) experienced related AEs of Grade \ge 3. These were mainly hypertension (10.5%), neutropenia (9.6%), anaemia (7.9%), neutrophil count decreased (4.0%), WBC count decreased, lymphopenia and blood creatine phosphokinase increased (3.1% each) and AST increased (2.0%).
L1400: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), the profile of the related AEs remains consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1401: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1402: The observed TEAEs and related TEAEs in all patients treated at 400 mg QD and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD were relevant to the mechanism of action of the study drug and have also been observed with other TKIs.
L1403: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1404: Below is an updated table presenting treatment-emergent adverse events occurring in \ge15% of patients in the population of patients with NSCLC based on the Applicant's 90-day update data. These adverse events were similar to results from the initial analysis. Additionally, AE profile was similar in patients with NSCLC versus all patients treated at 400 mg QD.

L1407: ## Laboratory Findings
L1408: ## Data:
L1409: ## *Hematology*
L1410: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, the median levels of leukocytes and neutrophils all decreased gradually after the start of treatment to week 3 or week 4 and then remained stable through the EOT in the study while platelets remained essentially unchanged from baseline to the EOT. Median hemoglobin and lymphocyte values increased slightly over baseline in the first 3- 4 weeks, then decreased and by the EOT median levels were below baseline for both hemoglobin (105.5 g/L) and for lymphocytes. Similar patterns were seen in the population of NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1411: ## *Serum Chemistry*
L1412: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, the median levels of ALT, AST, and total bilirubin remained essentially unchanged from baseline to the EOT. Median values of alkaline phosphate
L1413: increased slightly from baseline (86.0 IU/dL) to Week 2 (103 IU/dL), decreased gradually from Weeks 6 to 24, reaching a median value of 56.0 IU/dL, and then remained stable for the rest of the study. Median phosphate values decreased gradually over the course of study from baseline of 1.13 mmol/L to EOT 0.98 mmol/L. Similar patterns were seen for NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1414: ## **Coagulation**
L1415: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, adverse events associated with coagulation abnormalities were rare and included activated partial thromboplastin time prolonged (1 patient with a Grade 1, unrelated event) and international normalized ratio increased (1 patient with 1 related Grade 1 AE and 1 patient with 1 unrelated Grade 2 AE).
L1416: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1417: Generally, the laboratory abnormalities observed during the study in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were transient, resolved/resolved with sequelae with study drug interruption and/or supportive care, and not associated with significant clinical outcomes.
L1418: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1419: FDA agrees with the analyses of serum laboratory tests presented above. FDA also analyzed laboratory abnormalities for the all patients treated at 400 mg QD safety analysis population and the NSCLC population based on the 90 day update, and results were similar between the two populations. Results for patients with NSCLC treated at 400 mg QD are presented in the table below. Decreased albumin (all Grade 34%, no Grade 3-4) and decreased leukocytes (all grade 62%, grade 3-4 4.3%) are not included in the table below since Grade 1-2 decreased albumin is of limited clinical relevance and decreased leukocytes is better defined by specific values for decreased neutrophils and decreased lymphocytes, which are included in the table. There were no Grade 3-4 laboratory abnormalities with an incidence of \ge 2\% other than those listed in the table below.

L1424: ## Vital Signs
L1425: ## Data:
L1426: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, median blood pressure levels had increased above baseline at Week 1 of treatment for both systolic blood pressure (from 120.0 mmHg [range: 87 to 174] to 130.0 mmHg [range: 91 to 197]) and diastolic blood pressure (from 74 mmHg to 81.5 mmHg), and then systolic blood pressure remained close to 130.0 mmHg through Week 48 (while the N = 74); at the EOT the median systolic level was 120.0 mmHg and diastolic was 79.0 mmHg. This increase in blood pressure is reflected in the reports of hypertension as an AE (in 28.0% of all patients).
L1427: Among all patients treated at 400 mg QD, although median body temperature remained constant from baseline throughout the study, pyrexia was reported as an AE in 20.1% of all patients treated with 400 mg QD and was one of the most common AEs in the System Organ Class of General Disorders and Administration Site Conditions.
L1428: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), vital signs in all patients treated at 400 mg QD remains consistent with what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1429: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1430: Based on the frequency of hypertension events and the biological plausibility, a causal association to pralsetinib appears possible. The events were routinely managed by standard of care anti-hypertensive treatment resulting in event resolution.
L1431: Other than an increase in blood pressure, none of the assessments of vital signs indicated a clinically meaningful change in either all patients treated at 400 mg QD and the population of NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1432: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1433: FDA agrees. For assessment of hypertension see Significant Adverse Events.
L1434: ## **Electrocardiograms (ECGs)**
L1435: ## **Data:**
L1436: None of the abnormal ECG results in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were clinically significant at baseline except for 2 patients (&lt; 1%). Change from baseline for all doses/schedules showed a change for 5.4% to 13.8% of patients at any visit. At the EOT, change from baseline was observed for 1.1% of patients. Electrocardiogram QT prolongation as an AE was reported in 16 patients (4.5%) (10 related and 2 unrelated Grade 1 events, 2 related and 1 unrelated Grade 2 events, and 1 related Grade 3 event).
L1437: None of the abnormal ECG results in NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD were clinically significant at baseline except for 2 patients (1.1%). Over the course of the study, 4.5% to 14.0% of patients experienced a change at any timepoint. At the EOT, change from baseline was observed for &lt; 1% of patients.
L1438: There were no clinically meaningful differences in change from baseline in ECG results between patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=354) and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1439: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period, the abnormal ECG results among all patients treated at 400 mg QD were clinically significant for an additional 6 patients compared to what was reported in the initial NDA. Changes from baseline and deterioration from baseline at the end of treatment for all patients at 400 mg QD was similar to what was reported in the initial NDA. ECG QT prolongation as an AE was reported in 23 patients (5.3%) which was similar to what was
L1440: reported in the initial NDA.
L1441: There were no clinically meaningful differences in change from baseline in ECG results between patients treated at 400 mg QD (N=438) and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1442: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1443: Pralsetinib administration did not have any clinically meaningful or statistically significant effects on ECGs.
L1444: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1445: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L1446: ## QT
L1447: ## Data:
L1448: An analysis of continuous ECG (Holter) data from the Phase 2 part of the Study BLU-667-1101 was performed. A total of 34 patients were included in the QT/QTc population. The primary outcome measure for cardiac safety was an analysis of the regression relationship between \DeltaQTcF and the plasma concentration of pralsetinib at matching times post-dose.
L1449: Key observations from the QT cardiac assessment in Study BLU-667-1101 were as follows:
L1450: - The primary outcome measure indicated that pralsetinib had no clinically relevant or statistically significant effect on QT prolongation with a linear slope of -0.0003 ms/ng/mL and a p-value of 0.841;
- The secondary analysis results showed Least Squares Mean values of \DeltaQTcF \le 7.8 ms;
- No patients had a QTcF value &gt; 500 ms;
- Analysis of HR, PR interval, and QRS duration were likewise negative, i.e., there was no effect of pralsetinib treatment on these measures;
- Pralsetinib administration at 400 mg QD was observed to have no clinically relevant and statistically significant effects on the ECG or specifically was not associated with evidence for prolongation of the QTc interval.
L1451: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1452: Based on the results of ECG analyses, pralsetinib was not found to cause QT prolongation and there was no evidence of cardiac repolarization prolongation.
L1453: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1454: FDA agrees with Applicant's position.
L1455: ## **Immunogenicity**
L1456: Not applicable.
L1457: ## 8.2.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1458: The events of pneumonia, pneumonitis, and tumor lysis syndrome were identified as AESIs during the development of pralsetinib, based on the clinical experience taking into consideration multiple factors such as incidence, severity, relationship to study drug, medical impact, and clinical outcome of the AES.
L1459: ## 8.2.5.1 Pneumonia
L1460: ## Data:
L1461: Overall, 47 (13%) of the 354 patients in the safety population who started treatment at 400 mg QD reported events of pneumonia (referred to as grouped pneumonia events), including 40 patients (11%) who reported pneumonia, 3 patients (&lt;1%) who reported pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, and single reports (&lt;1%) of lung infection, atypical pneumonia, pneumonia bacterial, pneumonia cytomegaloviral, and pneumonia staphylococcal. Of these 47 patients, 34 (72%) reported events that were \ge Grade 3 in severity. Fourteen (4%) of the 354 patients reported grouped pneumonia events that were assessed as related to study drug. Thirty-four patients (10%) reported serious grouped pneumonia events, of which 10 patients (3%) reported serious grouped pneumonia events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1462: The 10 serious related pneumonia events included 8 patients who had NSCLC and 2 patients who had thyroid cancer. The events included 7 patients with pneumonia and 3 patients with pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia. All 10 patients had confounders/risk factors including NSCLC which is known to be associated with an increased risk of pulmonary infections, pre-existing history of diabetes, asthma, hilar adenopathy, recent external beam radiation around/near the chest area, former smoking, concurrent SAEs of sepsis, and ongoing lymphopenia or neutropenia at or around the time of the pneumonia event. In addition, 3 pneumonia events were caused by opportunistic infections (pneumocystis jirovecii), and all 3 of these patients had lymphopenia at the time of the event.
L1463: Of the 179 patients with NSCLC who initiated treatment at 400 mg QD, 29 patients (16%) reported grouped events of pneumonia, including 11 patients (6%) reported grouped pneumonia events that were assessed as related to study drug. Twenty patients (11%) with NSCLC who initiated treatment at 400 mg QD reported serious grouped pneumonia events, of which 8 patients (4.5%) reported serious related pneumonia events. One patient with NSCLC who initiated study drug treatment at 400 mg QD reported Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia.
L1464: In general, there were no clinically meaningful differences between all patients treated at 400 mg QD and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1465: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), 60 (14%) of the 438 patients in the safety population who started treatment at 400 mg QD reported events of pneumonia, including 48 patients (11%) who reported pneumonia, 3 patients (&lt;1%) who reported pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, 2 patients each (&lt;1%) who reported lung infection and pneumonia bacterial, and single patients who reported pneumonia influenza, atypical pneumonia, pneumonia cytomegaloviral, pneumonia haemophilus, pneumonia moraxella, pneumonia staphylococcal and pneumonia streptococcal. Sixteen (4%) of the 438 patients reported grouped pneumonia events that were assessed as related to study drug. Forty-one patients (9%) reported serious grouped pneumonia events, of which 12 patients (3%) reported serious grouped pneumonia events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1466: No notable differences were seen through the update period compared to what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1467: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1468: The incidence of pneumonia and grouped pneumonia reported in patients treated at 400 mg QD in Study BLU-667-1101 was consistent with what one would expect with a TKI treatment for this population of patients with advanced NSCLC (Burotto et al, 2015; Inoue et al, 2003).
L1469: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1470: Patients in the NSCLC population and patients who have metastases to the lungs do have a higher risk for developing pneumonia; however, it cannot be ruled out that pralsetinib contributed to pneumonia. In the safety population of 438 patients treated with pralsetinib 400 mg QD, the incidence of pneumonia was 14%. Among patients with adverse reaction of pneumonia, 1.4% of patients permanently discontinued pralsetinib, 6% had treatment interruptions and 1.6% had dose reductions (see Table 54 Adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation 90 day update, Table 55 Adverse events leading to treatment interruption 90-day update, Table 56 Adverse events leading to dose reduction 90-day update). Five deaths due to pneumonia were reported (refer to Table 46 Original NDA and 90-Day Update: Death within 30 Days of Last Pralsetinib Dose).
L1471: ## 8.2.5.2 Pneumonitis
L1472: ## Data:
L1473: Overall, 35 (10%) of the 354 patients in the safety population who started treatment at 400 mg QD reported events of pneumonitis. Of these 35 patients, 9 (26%) reported events that were ≥ Grade 3 in severity. Thirty (9%) of the 354 patients reported pneumonitis events that were assessed as related to study drug. Fourteen patients (4%) reported serious pneumonitis events, of which 11 patients (3%) reported serious pneumonitis events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1474: The 14 serious pneumonitis events included 8 patients who had NSCLC and 6 patients who had thyroid cancer. Eleven of the 14 patients had confounders/risk factors that included prior history of pneumonitis that predated initiation of BLU-667, external beam radiation to the chest/back/spine area, or prior treatment with chemotherapeutic regimens before initiating BLU-667 that were known to be associated with causing pneumonitis (e.g. Pemetrexed, Pembrolizumab, Vandetanib, Docetaxel, Osimertinib).
L1475: Seventeen of the 179 patients with NSCLC who initiated treatment at 400 mg QD (9%) reported pneumonitis events that were assessed as related to study drug. Seven patients (4%) with NSCLC who initiated treatment at 400 mg QD reported serious pneumonitis events. In general, there were no clinically meaningful differences between patients treated at 400 mg QD and
L1476: NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD.
L1477: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), 45 (10%) of the 438 patients in the safety population who started treatment at 400mg QD reported grouped events of pneumonitis (including PTs of pneumonitis and interstitial lung disease), including 43 patients who reported events of pneumonitis and 2 patients who reported events of interstitial lung disease. Forty (9%) of the 438 patients reported grouped pneumonitis events that were assessed as related to study drug. Twenty-two patients (5%) reported serious grouped pneumonitis events, of which 19 patients (4%) reported serious grouped pneumonitis events that were assessed as related to study drug.
L1478: No notable differences were seen through the Day 90 Safety Update period compared to what was reported in the initial NDA.
L1479: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1480: The incidence of pneumonitis reported in patients treated at 400 mg QD in Study BLU-667-1101 was generally consistent with a TKI treatment for this population of patients with advanced NSCLC (Burotto et al, 2015; Inoue et al, 2003). In addition, among the patients with related SAEs of pneumonitis (3%) in this population, the majority had received prior therapies (before initiation of pralsetinib) that are known to be associated with causing pneumonitis (e.g., pemtrexed, pembrolizumab, vandetanib, docetaxel, osimertinib).
L1481: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1482: Among the 438 patients in the safety population of patients treated with pralsetinib 400 mg QD, 43 patients (10%) experienced Grade 1-5 events of pneumonitis, 10 Grade 3 events and 2 Grade 4 events (grade 3-4, 2.7%). There were two deaths (0.5%) attributed to pneumonitis based on FDA's review of death narratives (see section 8.2.4). Pneumonitis/ILD is a known serious adverse event of tyrosine kinase inhibitors and requires appropriate management. FDA requested the Applicant to complete additional analyses of patients who experienced Grade 1 and 2 pneumonitis events to explore if continuing pralsetinib after dose reduction would be appropriate. The Applicant's analysis is presented below:

L1487: ## Pralsetinib
L1488: Based on the above information, there were patients with Grade 1-2 pneumonitis who were rechallenged with pralsetinib and did not experience recurrence of pneumonitis. It seems acceptable to withhold pralsetinib until resolution of pneumonitis and re-challenge a patient with pralsetinib at a reduced dose if they experience Grade 1 or 2 pneumonitis; however, it will be recommended that pralsetinib be discontinued if pneumonitis recurs. These dosage modification recommendations will be included in section 2 of the USPI and pneumonitis will be included in Section 5 of the USPI.
L1489: ## 8.2.5.3 Tumor Lysis Syndrome
L1490: ## Data:
L1491: In the overall safety population, tumor lysis syndrome was reported in 2 patients as a related Grade 3 SAE, both of whom had Stage IV MTC. One patient started treatment at 300 mg QD and 1 patient started treatment at 200 mg BID.
L1492: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), In addition to the 2 cases of Grade 3 tumor lysis syndrome reported in the initial NDA, 1 non-serious related AE (Grade 1) in 1 patient with medullary thyroid cancer treated at 400 mg QD was reported during the D90 Safety Update period.
L1493: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1494: Tumor lysis is rare in patients with solid tumors and is thus not commonly seen in patients with NSCLC; tumor lysis is most common in patients with hematological malignancies with rapid cellular death in response to treatment (Mirrakhimov et al, 2014).
L1495: (b) (4)
L1496: (b) (4)
L1497: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1498: Tumor lysis syndrome only occurred in 3 patients and 2 out of the three patients experienced a Grade 3 event, while one patient experienced a nonserious event of tumor lysis syndrome. For the event categorized as non-serious, the patient had a rapid clinical response to hydration and lacked an elevated uric acid level it was reasonable for the Applicant to consider this as a nonserious case. At least one case required administration of rasburicase and allopurinol and prompted hospitalization.
L1499: (b) (4)
L1500: (b) (4)

L1515: Please see tables below for safety analyses by demographic subgroup. FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position that there were no clinically meaningful differences in safety between the subgroups based on age. The incidence of grade ≥3 TEAEs and serious TEAEs were approximately 20% higher in the subgroup of patients age ≥65 years old than in the subgroup &lt;65 years old, and the incidence of discontinuation of pralsetinib for TEAE was approximately 10% higher.

L1526: ## 8.2.9. **Additional Safety Explorations**
L1527: ## **Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development**
L1528: ## **Data:**
L1529: No clinical carcinogenicity studies have been conducted.
L1530: In patients treated at 400 mg QD, 15 of 354 patients (4.2%) experienced AEs within the MedDRA System Organ Class of Neoplasms Benign, Malignant and Unspecified. Tumor pain was reported in 4 patients, basal cell carcinoma, cancer pain, lipoma, MTC and anaplastic large-cell lymphoma in 2 patients each. Hemangioma of skin, malignant neoplasm progression and melanocytic naevus were reported in 1 patient each.
L1531: Through Day 90 Safety Update period (13Feb2020), the number of patients with events was updated to 14 patients (3.2%).
L1532: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1533: No clinical carcinogenicity studies have been conducted with pralsetinib. In Study BLU-667-1101, TEAEs within the MedDRA System Organ Class of Neoplasms Benign, Malignant and Unspecified were reported in patients treated with pralsetinib. Currently, limited long-term data preclude definitive evaluation of carcinogenicity.
L1534: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1535: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted with pralsetinib. Consistent with the principles of the ICH S9 guidance, carcinogenicity studies are not necessary to support the use of pralsetinib in patients with advanced NSCLC.
L1536: If assessing tumor development, the only AEs from the Applicant's list above which would be considered relevant to such an assessment are basal cell carcinoma and anaplastic large cell lymphoma. The currently available data do not raise concern for a safety signal; however, as noted by the Applicant, there are no long-term data available to allow definitive evaluation of the risk of tumor development.
L1537: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L1538: ## **Data:**
L1539: There were no positive pregnancy test results and no pregnancies were reported during Study BLU-667-1101.
L1540: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1541: No clinical reproductive and developmental toxicity studies have been conducted and the
L1542: teratogenic potential of pralsetinib has not been established.
L1543: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1544: FDA agrees that no clinical reproductive and developmental toxicity studies have been conducted with pralsetinib; however, based on findings from animal studies and its mechanism of action, pralsetinib can cause fetal harm when administered to a pregnant woman. Oral administration of pralsetinib to pregnant rats during the period of organogenesis resulted in malformations and embryolethality at maternal exposures below the human exposure at the clinical dose of 400 mg once daily.
L1545: This information will be included under the heading of Embryo-Fetal Toxicity in Section 5 (Warnings and Precautions) and in Section 8.1 (Pregnancy) of the USPI for pralsetinib, along with instructions to advise pregnant women of the potential risk to a fetus. The USPI will also instruct providers to advise females and males of reproductive potential to use effective non-hormonal contraception during treatment with pralsetinib and for 2 weeks after the final dose.

L1553: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1554: ## Data:
L1555: No cases of overdose have been reported. The highest dose of pralsetinib studied clinically is 600 mg QD.
L1556: No cases of drug abuse have been reported with pralsetinib.
L1557: No formal studies of withdrawal or rebound effects associated with pralsetinib treatment have been conducted. No cases of withdrawal or rebound have been reported with pralsetinib.
L1558: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1559: There is no known antidote for pralsetinib overdose. In the event of suspected overdose, pralsetinib dosing should be interrupted, supportive care instituted, and the patient observed
L1560: ## Pralsetinib
L1561: until clinical stabilization.
L1562: Because pralsetinib is not pharmacologically or structurally related to drugs known to have abuse potential, drug abuse, withdrawal, and rebound with pralsetinib is unlikely.
L1563: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1564: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L1574: ## **Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting**
L1575: ## Data:
L1576: The 404 patients included in the overall safety population of Study BLU-667-1101 and 354 patients treated at 400 mg QD are considered adequate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on AE management.
L1577: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1578: Safety of pralsetinib in the postmarket setting is not expected to differ significantly from that observed in Study BLU-667-1101 as the study population is reflective of the patients expected to be treated with pralsetinib in the postmarket setting.
L1579: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1580: Pralsetinib is expected to be administered by oncologists; management of and monitoring for adverse effects of anti-cancer medications, including potentially serious adverse effects, is standard practice in oncology. FDA does not anticipate that the safety of pralsetinib will differ significantly in the post-market setting significantly.
L1581: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1582: ## **Data:**
L1583: The safety analyses in all patients treated at 400 mg QD in Study BLU-667-1101 demonstrated that pralsetinib treatment is generally well-tolerated.
L1584: In the safety population, median (range) treatment duration was 5.52 (0.1, 22.3) months for all patients treated at all doses/schedules. The key safety results for all patients treated at 400 mg QD were:
L1585: - 339 patients (94.4%) experienced any AEs. The most common AE (in &gt; 25% of patients) was AST increased (38.7%), followed by constipation (34.2%), anaemia (31.9%), diarrhoea (27.7%), ALT increased (27.1%), and hypertension (28.0%). Overall, 232 patients (65.5%) experienced any \ge Grade 3 AEs;
- 320 patients (90.4%) experienced AEs related to pralsetinib. The most common related AEs (in &gt; 20.0% of patients) were AST increased (31.4%), anaemia (21.8%), ALT increased (21.5%), constipation (21.2%), and hypertension (20.3%). A total of 155 patients (43.8%) experienced related \ge Grade 3 AEs;
- 156 patients (44.1%) experienced any SAEs. The most common SAEs (in &gt; 2 patients) were pneumonia (8.2%), disease progression (5.1%), pneumonitis (4.0%), urinary tract infection (2.8%), and sepsis and pyrexia (2.5% each). A total of 58 patients (16.4%) experienced related SAEs. The most common related SAE was pneumonitis (3.1%). Overall, 40 patients (9.1%) died due to AE during the study (&lt; 1% due to related AE);
- 3 patients (&lt;1.0%) had \ge 1 dose escalation and 215 (60.7%) had \ge 1 dose reduction, interruption, or missing dose due to an AE during the study. A total of 58 patients (16.4%) experienced AEs as primary or contributing reason leading to permanent treatment discontinuation (including “disease progression” reported as an AE term and AEs that represented symptoms of disease progression). The most common AEs (in &gt; 1% of patients) leading to treatment discontinuation were disease progression (3.4%), pneumonia (1.4%), and pneumonitis (1.1%);
- AESIs:40 patients (11.3%) experienced \ge 1 AESI of pneumonia (related in 2.8% of patients, SAEs in 8.2%, and leading to permanent treatment discontinuation in 1.4%);
- 35 patients (9.9%) experienced \ge 1 AESI of pneumonitis, related in 11.0% of patients, SAEs in 4.0%, and leading to permanent treatment discontinuation in 1.1%);
- Tumor lysis syndrome was not reported in any patient who was treated at 400 mg QD;
- Generally, the laboratory abnormalities observed during the study were transient resolved/resolved with sequelae following study drug interruption and/or supportive care, and not associated with significant clinical outcomes.
L1586: ## Pralsetinib
L1587: - Pralsetinib administration did not have any clinically meaningful nor statistically significant effects on ECGs and was not associated with evidence of cardiac repolarization prolongation.
L1588: The safety analyses in all patients treated at 400 mg QD and NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD in Study BLU-667-1101 demonstrated that pralsetinib treatment at a dose of 400 mg in a QD schedule is appropriate for this patient population. This safety profile is consistent with the overall safety profile of all patients in the study.
L1589: For the 179 NSCLC patients treated at 400 mg QD in the safety population, median (range) treatment duration was 5.16 (0.2 to 19.8) months. The key safety results for these patients were:
L1590: - 169 patients (94.4%) experienced any AEs. The most common AE (reported in &gt; 25% of patients) was AST increased (41.3%), followed by constipation (32.4%), anaemia and ALT increased (28.5% each), and hypertension (25.7%). Overall, 114 patients (63.7%) experienced any \ge Grade 3 AEs;
- 161 (89.9%) experienced AEs related to pralsetinib. The most common related AE was AST increased (35.2%), followed by ALT increased (24.6%), and constipation and anaemia (20.1% each). A total of 78 patients (43.6%) experienced related \ge Grade 3 AEs. The most common \ge Grade 3 AE was hypertension (12.8%), followed by anaemia and neutropenia (11.2% each);
- 91 patients (50.8%) experienced any SAEs. The most common SAEs (occurring in &gt; 2% of patients) were pneumonia (10.1%), disease progression (6.7%), pneumonitis and sepsis (3.9% each), and anaemia, hypertension, pyrexia, and urinary tract infection (2.2% each). A total of 36 patients (20.1%) experienced SAEs related to pralsetinib. The most common related SAEs (occurring in &gt; 1 patient) were pneumonia (3.9%), pneumonitis (3.4%), sepsis, anaemia, and hypertension (1.7% each), and neutropenia (1.1%). Overall, 19 patients (10.6%) died due to AE during the study (none were due to related AE);
- 1 patient (&lt; 1%) had any dose escalation and 104 (58.1%) had any dose reduction, interruption, or missing dose due to AE during the study. A total of 117 patients (65.4%) experienced AEs leading to dose modification and 35 (19.6%) experienced AEs as primary or contributing reason leading to permanent treatment discontinuation (including “disease progression” reported as an AE term and AEs that represented symptoms of disease progression). The most common AEs leading to treatment discontinuation (reported in &gt; 1% of patients) were disease progression (4.5%), pneumonia (2.2%), pneumonitis (1.7%), and dyspnoea and sepsis (1.1% each);
- AESIs: there were no clinically meaningful differences in AESIs between these patients and all patients treated at 400 mg QD;
- Laboratory abnormalities were similar to those observed in all patients treated at 400 mg QD: transient, and resolved/resolved with sequelae following study drug interruption and/or supportive care and not associated with significant clinical
L1591: outcomes.
L1592: Through the Day 90 Safety Update period (cutoff date 13 February 2020), the safety profile of pralsetinib remained consistent with that reported in the initial NDA submission (data cutoff date of 18 November 2019).
L1593: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1594: Treatment with 400 mg QD oral pralsetinib in 354 patients with RET-fusion positive NSCLC, MTC and other solid tumors was associated with a generally well-tolerated, predictable, and manageable safety profile, allowing for prolonged treatment in patients with unresectable, metastatic NSCLC, regardless of prior line of treatment. The most common AEs (in &gt; 25% of patients) in all patients treated at 400 mg QD were AST increased (38.7%), followed by constipation (34.2%), anaemia (31.9%), diarrhoea (27.7%), ALT increased (27.1%), and hypertension (28.0%). The proportion of patients who experienced TEAEs of ≥ Grade 3 (65.5%) regardless of causality was typical for studies in patients with advanced NSCLC and most were manageable, reversible, and seldom led to treatment discontinuation. The most common treatment-emergent SAEs (&gt; 2% of patients) were pneumonia (8.2%), disease progression (5.1%), pneumonitis (4.0%), urinary tract infection (2.8%), and sepsis and pyrexia (2.5% each). The most common related AEs (reported in &gt; 20% of patients) as assessed by the Investigators were AST increased (31.4%), ALT increased (21.5%), anaemia (21.8%), and hypertension (20.3%), and the most common related SAEs (occurring in &gt; 2 patients) were pneumonitis (3.1%), pneumonia (2.0%), and anaemia, hypertension, neutropenia, pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia, sepsis, and thrombocytopenia (&lt; 1% each).
L1595: In general, the risks of treatment with pralsetinib were less than what these RET-fusion positive patients would have been exposed to by treatment with the non-selective, intravenous, standard of care agents currently available. In particular, pneumonitis has been reported to occur in up to 19% of patients treated with immune checkpoint inhibitors and is higher when used in combination with other therapies (Suresh et al, 2018). In comparison, 10.6% of NSCLC patients treated with 400 mg QD reported pneumonitis. Additionally, dermatological events commonly seen with other treatments (30% to 40% with rash and pruritus in patients treated with nivolumab or pembrolizumab; Naidoo et al, 2015) were less common with pralsetinib 400 mg QD in these RET-fusion positive NSCLC patients (6.7% with rash and 2.8% with pruritus).
L1596: Due to the embryofetal toxicity associated with 'on-target' RET inhibition, pralsetinib should not be used in pregnant women.
L1597: While pralsetinib is a selective RET inhibitor, there are other kinases; it can inhibit at lower levels that may result in some 'off-target' toxicities. Some of the toxic effects seen in nonclinical studies with pralsetinib were the result of effects on off-target VEGFR, JAK2, and/or fibroblast growth factor receptor proteins. The AE profile seen in humans, which included hypertension
L1598: ## Pralsetinib
L1599: (i.e., VEGF) and abnormalities in hematological parameters (i.e., JAK2), was consistent with these nonclinical observations. In the clinical Study BLU-667-1101 at the dose of 400 mg QD, some of the most common AEs were hypertension (28.0% of patients), anemia (31.9%), and neutropenia (19.8 %), which were also the most common \ge Grade 3 AEs (13.8% for hypertension, 12.1% for anaemia, and 10.5% for neutropenia). These effects on blood pressure and blood counts are known to be reversible and manageable. Reduction in neutrophils and lymphocytes is associated with an increased risk for lung infections, which is why patients should also be monitored for signs and symptoms of pneumonia or other lung infections.
L1600: Pralsetinib did not result in QT prolongation in the clinical setting, it did not have any clinically meaningful or statistically significant effects on the ECG, and specifically it was not associated with evidence of cardiac repolarization prolongation. Thus, there is no significant risk of QT prolongation expected with the treatment of pralsetinib in patients.
L1601: The overall safety experience suggests that pralsetinib has a favorable tolerability profile in advanced cancer patients in relation to current available therapies. The risks associated with pralsetinib are readily detected and commonly seen with TKIs and/or in association with the underlying disease and were mostly of low severity and without significant impact on the course of treatment. Management of these events is part of standard clinical practice, including supportive medications, dose interruption, and potential dose reduction, and seldom led to treatment discontinuation. These risks are considered acceptable in relation to the severity and clinical outcome of the indication being treated.
L1602: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1603: FDA agrees with the description of the safety database provided by the Applicant and the Applicant's statement that through the Day 90 Safety Update period, the safety profile of pralsetinib remained consistent with that reported in the initial NDA submission.
L1604: In addition to pneumonitis identified by the Applicant for inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI, FDA identified the following additional adverse reactions for inclusion based on severity, incidence, and/or plausible mechanism of action: hepatotoxicity, hemorrhagic events, hypertension, and delayed wound healing.
L1605: FDA agrees with the Applicant's statement that management of adverse reactions reported with pralsetinib are part of standard clinical practice for oncologists, and the toxicity profile of pralsetinib is acceptable when considering the seriousness of the disease pralsetinib will be treating.
L1606: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1607: ## 8.3. **Statistical Issues**
L1608: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1609: There were no major statistical issues in the review of this NDA. However, the single-arm design of the main trial supporting efficacy may limit the interpretation of results, as a randomized comparison to standard of care for this disease setting is not available to provide context for the observed patient data. This is particularly problematic for time-to-event endpoints, such as PFS and OS, given the absence of an active control or comparison to the natural history of disease.
L1610: ## 8.4. **Conclusions and Recommendations**
L1611: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1612: Based on the review of clinical data from Study BLU-667-1101, the clinical review team recommends accelerated approval under the provisions of 21 CFR subpart H of this NDA for pralsetinib for the treatment of patients with RET-fusion positive metastatic NSCLC.
L1613: The basis of this recommendation is the favorable benefit: risk profile for pralsetinib as a single agent in patients with RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC. In the subgroup of patients who have previously received platinum chemotherapy (N=87) the ORR per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by a BIRC was 57% (95% CI 46, 68). Tumor responses were durable, with 80% having responses lasting \ge 6 months. In the population of patients with treatment-naïve RET fusion-positive metastatic NSCLC (N=27), the ORR was 70% (95% CI 50, 86), with 58% having responses lasting \ge 6 months.
L1614: The safety review of pralsetinib included data from 438 patients from ARROW. The primary risks identified as warranting inclusion in the Warnings and Precautions section of the USPI for pralsetinib are interstitial lung disease/pneumonitis, hypertension, hepatotoxicity, hemorrhagic events, and potential for risk of impaired wound healing. These risks are described in detail in this review and are addressed in product labeling. Overall the toxicity profile of pralsetinib is considered acceptable when assessed in the context of the treatment of this life-threatening disease.
L1615: Data to verify the clinical benefit will consist of additional data from the single arm trial, Study BLU-667-1101 (ARROW). Blueprint has also initiated a randomized, controlled trial of pralsetinib compared to platinum-based chemotherapy with or without pembrolizumab as first-line treatment for patients with metastatic RET fusion-positive NSCLC (Study BLUE-667-2303; AcceleRET Lung), and submission of data from this trial will be requested as a post-marketing commitment to provide additional information regarding outcomes with pralsetinib in the treatment-naïve patient population.

L1645: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L1646: The FDA's Assessment:
L1647: The clinical review team determined that a risk evaluation and mitigation strategy (REMS) was not required to ensure safe and effective use of pralsetinib for the indicated population given the current safety profile of pralsetinib and the experience of the medical oncology community in monitoring and managing adverse reactions related to targeted therapies, based on use of other marketed products of the same class. Recommendations for the safe and effective use of pralsetinib will be made in labeling, including a patient package insert (PPI). There are no additional risk management strategies required beyond the recommended labeling.
L1648: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1649: The FDA's Assessment: Blueprint has agreed to the following postmarketing requirements (PMR) and postmarketing commitments (PMC). Please refer to the approval letter for expected completion dates.
L1650: ## Clinical PMRs
L1651: ## PMR 3916-1:
L1652: Submit the final report, including datasets, from an ongoing clinical trial to verify and further characterize the clinical benefit of pralsetinib for the treatment of patients with 1) treatment-naïve RET fusion-positive NSCLC and with 2) RET fusion-positive NSCLC who have previously received platinum chemotherapy to provide a more precise estimation of the BICR-assessed overall response rate and duration of response after all responders in the population of patients with treatment-naïve NSCLC (approximately 120 patients) have been followed for at least 12 months from the date of initial response (or until disease progression, whichever comes first) and after all responders in the population of patients with NSCLC previously treated with platinum therapy (87 patients) have been followed for at least 6 months.
L1653: ## PMR 3916-(b)(4)
L1654: Conduct a comprehensive analysis evaluating and characterizing the incidence, clinical presentation, management, and outcome of the potential serious risk of pralsetinib associated gastrointestinal perforations and fistulas. Submit an integrated final report containing data from patient-level and pooled analyses of on-going and completed clinical trials, post-marketing reports and/or literature reports and a comprehensive pharmacovigilance assessment for this potential serious risk. The results from this report may inform product labeling.
L1655: ## Clinical Pharmacology PMRs
L1656: ## PMR 3916-3:
L1657: Conduct a clinical drug-drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of a P-gp inhibitor on the pharmacokinetics of pralsetinib and to inform appropriate dosing strategies for safe coadministration of pralsetinib with P-gp inhibitors. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled: "*Clinical Drug Interaction Studies - Cytochrome P450 Enzyme and Transporter- Mediated Drug Interactions*". Submit the datasets
L1658: with the final report. The results from this study may inform product labeling.
L1659: PMR 3916-(b)(4)
L1660: Conduct a physiologically-based pharmacokinetic modeling/simulation study to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor on the pharmacokinetics of pralsetinib, assess the magnitude of increased pralsetinib exposure, and inform appropriate dosing strategies for safe coadministration of pralsetinib with moderate CYP3A inhibitors. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled, "*In Vitro Drug Interaction Studies - Cytochrome P450 Enzyme and Transporter- Mediated Drug Interactions*" and "*Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Analyses – Format and Content*". Submit the model with the final report. The results from this study may inform product labeling.
L1661: PMR 3916-5:
L1662: Conduct a physiologically-based pharmacokinetic modeling/simulation study to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of a combined P-gp (b)(4) on the (b)(4), and inform appropriate dosing strategies for safe coadministration of pralsetinib with combined P-gp (b)(4) inhibitors. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled, "*In Vitro Drug Interaction Studies - Cytochrome P450 Enzyme and Transporter- Mediated Drug Interactions*" and "*Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Analyses – Format and Content*". Submit the model with the final report. The results from this study may inform product labeling.
L1663: PMR 3916-6:
L1664: Conduct a clinical drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of pralsetinib on the pharmacokinetics of transporter substrates of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE-1, and MATE-2K, assess the magnitude of exposure change, and inform appropriate dosing strategies for coadministration of pralsetinib with these transporter substrates. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled: "*Clinical Drug Interaction Studies - Cytochrome P450 Enzyme and Transporter- Mediated Drug Interactions*". Submit the datasets with final report. The results from this study may inform product labeling.
L1665: PMR 3916-7:
L1666: Conduct a clinical drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of pralsetinib on the pharmacokinetics of sensitive substrates of CYP3A4/5, CYP2C8, and CYP2C9, assess the magnitude of exposure change, and inform appropriate dosing strategies for safe coadministration of pralsetinib with sensitive substrates of CYP3A4/5, CYP2C8, and CYP2C9. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled: "*Clinical Drug Interaction Studies - Cytochrome P450 Enzyme and Transporter- Mediated Drug Interactions*". Submit the datasets with the final report. The results from this trial may inform
L1667: product labeling.
L1668: PMR 3916-8:
L1669: Conduct a hepatic impairment clinical trial to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of pralsetinib in subjects with moderate and severe hepatic impairment compared to subjects with normal hepatic function. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled "*Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling*". Submit the datasets with the final report. The results from this trial may inform product labeling.

L1812: ## 19.4. **OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)**
L1813: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1814: ## 19.4.1 **Population Pharmacokinetic Analyses**
L1815: The goal of population PK analysis (popPK) was to develop a population pharmacokinetic (PK) model to assess sources of variability (intrinsic and extrinsic covariates) of pralsetinib in healthy volunteers, patients with NSCLC and patients with thyroid cancer.
L1816: The population PK model for pralsetinib included 491 subjects (193 healthy subjects, 161 patients with NSCLC, 124 patients with RET-mutation positive MTC and 13 patients with RET-fusion positive thyroid) with 7566 quantifiable pralsetinib concentrations pooled from 5 clinical studies. A summary of the demographics and selected baseline lab assessments included in the popPK analysis was provided in Table 63 and Table 64.
L1817: The popPK analysis was conducted by the Applicant and evaluated by the pharmacometrics reviewer. The PK of pralsetinib was characterized by a one-compartment model with linear elimination. Absorption was described by a series of transit compartments (7, 4, and 7 transit compartments for capsule Process I, II, and III, respectively). Between-subject variability (BSV) on CL/F, V/F, and KTR was included. Between-occasion variability (BOV) on KTR and oral bioavailability (F) was included. An additive error model on the log-scale was used to describe the observed concentrations.
L1818: A stepwise covariate modeling approach was implemented to investigate the effects of covariates on pralsetinib. The most significant covariates in terms of \DeltaOBJ change were included in the forward inclusion model. From this model, all covariates that were previously identified as significant were re-investigated. This step was repeated until there were no more significant covariates to be included. From the full model, covariates were deleted one at a time in a backward elimination process. Covariates which could be deleted from the full model (without an associated increase in OBJ \ge 10.8 (p &lt; 0.001)) were sorted by OBJ and the covariate with the smallest \DeltaOBJ was removed from the model. This process was repeated until no more covariates can be removed from the model.
L1819: Parameter estimates of final model were provided in Table 65. The covariates included in the final model were as follows: weight on CL/F and V/F, patients with RET-altered thyroid cancer on KTR on Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1), patients with NSCLC on F, patients with NSCLC administered capsule Process I on F, age on CL/F, concomitant CYP3A4 inducer use on CL/F, and capsule Process on KTR.
L1820: The goodness of fit plots of model evaluation was presented in Figure 4. Plots of the observed versus population predicted concentrations demonstrated bias of fit in the low concentration range, but the majority of data are evenly scattered around the line of unity. No bias was found in other diagnostic plots, suggesting the model fitted the data with good precision and minimal bias in general. Prediction-corrected visual predictive check also showed that the final model adequately described the observed PK profile of pralsetinib across different tumor types (Figure 5).
L1821: The effects of all evaluated covariates on the pralsetinib exposure (AUC_{0-24hr,ss} and C_{max,ss}) were illustrated in the forest plot (Figure 6 and Figure 7). Overall, the magnitude of the effect of all covariates on pralsetinib exposure was within &lt;0.5-fold change compared to the reference
L1822: ## Pralsetinib
L1823: group, defined as patients with RET-altered thyroid cancer with median age (52 years) and weight (73.5 kg), with the 95% prediction interval (PI) overlapping with the 80-125% range. The post-hoc pralsetinib CL were similar between patients with normal liver function (N=241) and patients with mild hepatic impairment (N=42 (Figure 8). The post-hoc pralsetinib CL values were also similar between patients with normal renal function (N=121), patients with mild renal impairment (N=138) and patients with moderate renal function (N=26) (Figure 9).
L1824: These results suggest no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of pralsetinib were observed based on age (19 to 87 years), sex, race (White, Black, or Asian) and body weight (32.1 to 128.0 kg). Mild to moderate (CLCR 30 to 89 mL/min estimated by Cockcroft-Gault) renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le ULN and AST &gt; ULN or total bilirubin &gt; 1 to 1.5 times ULN and any AST) were found to have no effect on the exposure of pralsetinib.
L1825: The use of concomitant weak CYP3A4 inducers for 5 consecutive days was estimated to result in an approximately 20% reduction in Cmaxss and AUCss. The capsule Process III was found to achieve similar exposure compared to capsule Process II, while the capsule Process II was estimated to result in 32% higher exposure compared to capsule Process I.
L1826: The majority of healthy subjects or patients received a starting dose of 400 mg (N=400), a few healthy subjects in the analysis also received a dose of 200 mg (N=45), but very few patients with thyroid cancer received starting dose of 200 mg (N=9) or 300 mg (N=7). Dose-normalized concentrations are similar between 200 mg and 400 mg in healthy subjects, suggesting dose proportionality is observed in healthy volunteers. A small over proportional increase in dose-normalized concentration time profiles for the dose range between 200 mg – 400 mg is observed in patients with *RET*-fusion positive thyroid cancer. This finding is challenging to interpret given the small number of subjects on doses &lt;400 mg and is inconsistent with what was seen in the healthy volunteers.

L1858: ## 19.4.2 Exposure-Response Analyses
L1859: ## 1) Methods and Data
L1860: Exposure-response analyses were conducted by the Applicant to explore the relationship between exposure of pralsetinib and efficacy and safety in NSCLC patients who received pralsetinib.
L1861: The ER dataset contained all patients with valid safety and efficacy data and matching pralsetinib exposures. A total of 206 subjects were enrolled by the 18NOV2019 cutoff date with safety data. A total of 52 subjects were excluded as no pralsetinib PK was available (n=43) or subjects had prior RET therapy (n=9). Thus, 154 subjects with a matching pralsetinib exposure were included in the ER analysis for efficacy.
L1862: The ER analysis for efficacy explored the relationship between pralsetinib exposure and best overall response (BOR) and progression free survival (PFS).
L1863: The ER analysis for safety explored the relationship between pralsetinib exposure variables and the following measures of safety in the safety population.
L1864: - Grade 3+ any AE
- Grade 3+ pneumonia
- Grade 3+ pneumonitis
- Grade 3+ hypertension
- Grade 3+ lymphopenia
- Grade 3+ anemia
- Aspartate aminotransferase (AST): \Deltabaseline
- Alanine aminotransferase (ALT): \Deltabaseline
- Absolute neutrophil count (ANC): \Deltabaseline
- Hemoglobin: \Deltabaseline
- Lymphocytes: \Deltabaseline
- Systolic blood pressure: \Deltabaseline
- Diastolic blood pressure: \Deltabaseline
L1865: The primary exposure metrics for exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety assessment are model predicted average plasma concentration (C_{ave}) from the start of treatment until the first occurrence of an event of interest or censoring.
L1866: ## 2) Exposure-Efficacy Relationships
L1867: Kaplan-Meier curves of PFS by quartiles of C_{ave} are displayed in Figure 10. Overall, no clear trend was evident between PFS and pralsetinib exposure in the primary efficacy population. However, subjects with the highest exposure (4th quartile of C_{ave}) appeared to have the shortest PFS. This finding is likely biased due to many patients having dose reduction and drug holidays in the clinical trial. Patients with early progression are less likely to have dose holidays or dose reductions, and therefore will likely have a higher C_{ave}.
L1868: The average concentration up to the time of event (disease progression/death) or censoring is found to have no association with PFS based on a Cox proportional hazard model (P-value = 0.261). The baseline covariates including age group, BSA, geographic region, race, RET fusion, smoking history, gender, prior MKI therapy and prior platinum therapy were included in the hazard model. BSA, gender and baseline ECOG status were found to affect PFS.
L1869: Boxplots of C_{ave} by best overall response (BOR) was displayed in Figure 11. The distribution of C_{ave} appears to be similar across BOR. The proportion of CR/PR over the quartiles of C_{ave} was also displayed in Figure 12. No trend between BOR and quartiles of C_{ave} was evident. The baseline covariates across four exposure quartiles in the exposure-efficacy analyses appeared to be balanced (Table 66).

L1880: ## 2) Exposure-Safety Relationships
L1881: Time-to-event analysis suggests higher exposures of pralsetinib appeared to be correlated with the time to any grade 3+ adverse events, grade 3+ anemia AEs and grade 3+ pneumonia AEs (Figure 13 and Figure 14). Subjects in the 4th quartile of average exposure appeared to have a faster rate and higher probability of experiencing the first grade 3+ adverse event. The baseline covariates across four exposure quartiles in the exposure-safety analyses appeared to be balanced (Table 67). There was no clear relationship between exposure metrics and grade 3+ pneumonitis, hypertension, or lymphopenia AEs, but the analysis is limited as the rates of grade 3+ pneumonitis and lymphopenia were low.
L1882: Increases in pralsetinib exposure appeared to correlate with decreases in hemoglobin and ANC and minor increases in ALT (Figure 15). There did not appear to be any trend evident between pralsetinib and changes in AST, lymphocyte count or diastolic/systolic blood pressures.

L1892: ## 19.4.3 Bioanalytical Review
L1893: ## 19.4.3.1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1894: **Bioanalytical method review summary:** Validation for method BTM-2256-R0 was adequate for measurement of the concentration of pralsetinib in plasma to support studies BLU-667-0101, BLU-667-0102, BLU-667-0103, BLU-667-0104, BLU-667-0105, and BLU-667-1101. Validation for
L1895: method BTM-1132 was adequate for measurement of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in human urine to support study BLU-667-0104.
L1896: ## Method Description
L1897: ## 1. Determination of pralsetinib in plasma samples (method BTM-2256-R0)
L1898: A validated LC-MS/MS method was used for the determination of pralsetinib (X581238) in K2EDTA human plasma using isotopically labelled internal standard (X581238-[13C,2H3]). The method is suitable for measurement of the concentration of pralsetinib in plasma in studies BLU-667-0101, BLU-667-0102, BLU-667-0103, BLU-667-0104, BLU-667-0105, and BLU-667-1101. Both the method validation and sample analysis were performed at [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1899: The validation report and addenda (BLU-R6395, BLU-R6395A1, and BLU-R6395A2) and bioanalytical study reports for studies BLU-667-0101, BLU-667-0102, BLU-667-0103, BLU-667-0104, BLU-667-0105, and BLU-667-1101 (interim report) were reviewed. The Applicant's responses dated June 5, 2020; June 26, 2020; and July 31, 2020 to information requests were also reviewed. A summary of the method validation for measurement of pralsetinib is shown in Table 68 below.
L1900: In addition, review of an addendum validation report BLU-R6395A1 reveals stock and spiking solutions were stable at -20 °C for 182 days. Results of the long-term storage stability in matrix study demonstrated that pralsetinib was stable for 184 days when stored at temperatures of -20 °C and -70 °C. The long-term storage stability of pralsetinib in matrix at -70 °C was further extended to 802 days as stated in addendum validation report BLU-R6395A2.
L1901: In general, the bioanalytical method was fully validated and found to be sensitive and accurate for the determination of pralsetinib in human plasma samples. All samples were analyzed within the demonstrated stability window. In the responses dated June 26, 2020 and July 31, 2020 to information requests, Blueprint confirmed that the longest duration of sample storage for studies that were reviewed at the clinical site or central laboratory was 308 days and at the analytical site was 369 days with storage temperatures of approximately -75 °C to -70 °C at the clinical, central laboratory, and analytical sites.
L1902: ## 2. Determination of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in human urine (method BTM-1132)
L1903: A validated LC-MS/MS method was used for the determination of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in human urine using isotopically labelled internal standard (cortisol-d4)). The method is suitable for measurement of the concentration of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in urine in study BLU-667-0104. Both the method validation and sample analysis were performed at [REDACTED] (b) (4) The validation report ([REDACTED] (b) (4) -R824) and bioanalytical study report for study BLU-667-0104 were reviewed. A summary of the method validation for measurement of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol is shown in Table 69 below.
L1904: In addition, review of an addendum validation report (b) (4)-R824A1 revealed the cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol stock and spiking solutions were stable at 4 ^{\circ}C for 111 days and at room temperature for 14.5 hours (stock solution) and 6 hours (spiking solution). Results of the long-term storage stability study demonstrated that cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in human urine samples were stable at -20 ^{\circ}C for 207 days, covering the maximum storage of urine samples. Overall, the bioanalytical method was fully validated and found to be sensitive and accurate for the determination of cortisol and 6\beta-hydroxycortisol in human urine samples. All samples were analyzed within the demonstrated stability window.

L1912: ## Summary of method performance in analysis of clinical samples
L1913: The performance of the bioanalytical methods during the analysis of PK samples for individual clinical studies is summarized in Table 70. In general, the performance of methods BTM-2256 and BTM-1132 is adequate in analysis of the clinical samples in studies BLU-667-0101, BLU-667-
L1914: 0102, BLU-667-0103, BLU-667-0104, BLU-667-0105, and BLU-667-1101. Performances of the ISR batch runs for all studies were also acceptable.

L1932: Figure 1 displays the effect of Pralsetinib (X581238) on in vivo anti-tumor activity and RET signaling in mice bearing NSCLC CTG-0838 patient-derived xenografts.
The top panel is a line graph showing Mean Tumor Volume (\pm SEM) in \text{mm}^3 versus Day (0 to 30). The Vehicle Control group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 2000 \text{mm}^3 by Day 30. Treatment groups show varying degrees of inhibition:
BLU-83 (Cabo) 60 mg/kg QD: Tumor volume remains low, around 1000 \text{mm}^3.
X581238 (Pralsetinib) 3 mg/kg BID: Tumor volume remains low, around 500 \text{mm}^3.
X581238 10 mg/kg BID: Tumor volume remains low, around 300 \text{mm}^3.
X581238 30 mg/kg BID: Tumor volume remains low, around 200 \text{mm}^3.
X581238 60 mg/kg QD: Tumor volume remains low, around 100 \text{mm}^3.
The bottom panel is an immunoblot analysis showing pY1062-RET, pY239/pY240 Shc, and Total RET levels in tumor lysates collected at 4, 12, or 24 hours after the last dose on Day 4. The analysis compares Vehicle, Cabozantinib 60mg/kg QD, BLU-667 3mg/kg BID, BLU-667 10mg/kg BID, BLU-667 30mg/kg BID, and BLU-667 60mg/kg QD. The pY1062-RET signal is significantly reduced in all treated groups compared to Vehicle, indicating effective RET inhibition.
BLU-83 = cabozantinib; X581238 = pralsetinib; QD = once daily; BID = twice daily; tumor lysates for immunoblotting were collected at 4, 12, or 24 hours after the last dose on Day 4; (Applicant Figure reproduced from Study #1110-003 and #BPM-0020)
L1933: Figure 2 displays two Kaplan-Meier survival curves (A and B) illustrating the effect of Pralsetinib (BLU-667) on survival in RET-driven intracranial tumor models in mice.
A: Survival curves for Ba/F3-KIF5B-RET-luc model. The X-axis represents Days post inoculation (day), and the Y-axis represents Percent survival. Four groups are compared: Vehicle p.o. BID*42D (black line), BLU-667 3mg/kg p.o. BID*42D (red line), BLU-667 10mg/kg p.o. BID*42D (green line), and BLU-667 30mg/kg p.o. BID*42D (blue line). The 30 mg/kg dose shows the highest survival, reaching approximately 85% survival at 40 days, compared to 100% survival for the vehicle group at 10 days.
B: Survival curves for CR2518 CRC PDX model. The X-axis represents Treatment Days, and the Y-axis represents Percent survival (%). Three groups are compared: Vehicle (black line), BLU-667 10 mg/kg BID (red line), and BLU-667 30 mg/kg BID (green line). The 30 mg/kg dose shows significantly improved survival compared to the vehicle and 10 mg/kg doses, reaching approximately 90% survival at 90 days, compared to 50% survival for the vehicle group at 90 days.

Middle Right Plot: CWRES vs Population Prediction (ng/mL). The CWRES (Conditional Weighted Residuals) values are scattered around the zero line (red line), indicating a good fit. The y-axis ranges from -6 to 6, and the x-axis ranges from 0 to 3000 ng/mL.

L1948: Figure 17 displays seven scatterplots showing the change from baseline (ΔBaseline) for various safety endpoints (AST, ALT, ANC, HGB, LYM, D/ABP, SYSBP) plotted against the Cave concentration (ng/mL). Each plot includes a fitted regression line (orange).
ΔBaseline AST (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -100 to 200. The regression line is slightly positive.
ΔBaseline ALT (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -50 to 100. The regression line is slightly positive.
ΔBaseline ANC (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -10 to 10. The regression line is slightly negative.
ΔBaseline HGB (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -80 to 40. The regression line is slightly negative.
ΔBaseline LYM (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -1.5 to 1.5. The regression line is slightly negative.
ΔBaseline D/ABP (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -50 to 50. The regression line is slightly positive.
ΔBaseline SYSBP (IU/L) vs. Cave (ng/mL): The Y-axis ranges from -60 to 30. The regression line is slightly positive.
